# State

The live record of where the project is. **Every change updates this file in the same commit** (rule 10 in
`CLAUDE.md`), so any agent or session can pick up from here without reading the history.

How to update it:
- **Systems:** keep one short entry per system current. Change it when the system changes.
- **In progress:** add a line when you start something that will outlast your session or that another agent might
  touch; remove it when done. Say who (agent or session), what, which files, and since when.
- **Changes:** add a dated entry at the top for every change: what changed, the main files, what was checked
  (tests, shots), and who made it. Keep each entry a few lines; detail belongs in the commit or the code.

| Walker | **Walk, twin-stick (Keith 2026-10-31: "move in the direction you press"; keep the look and the sprint's drift):** the body's velocity follows the stick (`walkSteer`: 30 m/s² along it, 40 braking the rest, × (legs' accel/4)^0.5), walk speed in 0.18 s, a 90° change 0.18 s, a reversal 0.33 s, a stop 0.14 s (were 1.36 / 0.70 / 1.52 / 0.91); the legs turn after the travel at 360°/s and never gate it; feet land ahead along the travel on their own side; more than 125° from the aim the legs keep facing it and step backwards (0.9 speed); steps hurry after hard turns (`crossHurry`, `turnHurry`) so feet keep up; overspeed handed on by the sprint rolls off at the old 6 m/s² with the stick held, up to the Shift run; past it the feet slide. **Grip by speed (Keith 2026-10-31: "as the velocity reduces, the traction returns"; `gripAt`):** sideways grip is the walk brake (40 m/s²) up to the Shift run + `gripFullOver`, the sprint's 0.7 g at the legs' top and past it while sprinting, `gripCurve` (2) between; let go of Space and the curve runs out to the burst's 20 m/s (`gripLetGoOver`), so grip comes back from the moment of letting go (8 m/s² at 18 m/s, 40 by 8.5). Any skid (after a sprint, a dash, a landing) is steered with the walk at that grip, no window by time (was a flat 3 m/s²); run-on with the stick held 1.0 s / 12 m (was 3.1 s / 34 m). Sliders in Move; `dash-check` asserts it. `turnRate` is the sprint's only. Sliders in Move; `tools/response-check.ts`. **Sprint (Keith 2026-10-26, gait redone after his 18:28 playtest):** with `bound` on (Space held through the dash, `src/mech/bound.ts`) the walk blends into a run: even left/right footfalls (3 steps/s rising to 3.6), short flights that grow with speed, hips bobbing 0.05 m, sprinter swings (follow-through, a tuck with the thigh up and toes curled, a reach and a plant under the hips), the hock opening further at push-off, the hull kept 2.35 m over the highest ground ahead; building toward the legs' `boundTop` (Rampart 14 m/s); grip-limited turning, drift and skid, little steering in the air. Running off a crest flies on the gallop's air pose and lands on its staggered stance, then runs on (the old whole-gallop gait deleted 2026-11-07). Sprint sliders. **Weapons off sprinting (Keith 21:54):** after `sprintGunsAfter` (0.6 s) the weapons stop and the torso faces the travel line, hull leaning forward; back on release (`Bound.gunsOff`). **Lean into turns (21:54):** the whole mech rolls by the lateral pull (speed × the run line's turn rate, `turnRoll` × the true lean, capped at `turnRollMax` 30°), the hull fully (shoulders over the inside), rolled about the outside hip with the hips `turnLeanShift` toward the inside, so the inside leg folds short (`sim.hullRoll`). **Climbing at a run (21:54):** quicker, shorter strides with the grade (`runClimbSteps`), the hips never lifted past the reach of the feet carrying them (`runReachGive`). **Whip (21:54):** the run's swing hangs behind, then whips up toward the tuck (`runWhip`, `runWhipAt`). **Footfalls (21:54):** each plant compresses (hips and nose dip), the push-off extends, throws sand back and digs the toes; shake and thump per footfall; a sprinting leg at full stretch drags its toes on (2026-10-27). **Feet sink (21:54):** planted feet (and, walking, the hips with them) sit into soft ground by surface (`src/mech/sink.ts`: sand 0.175 m, snow 0.22, rock 0), `WalkerSim.sinkAt`. **Dash (Keith 2026-10-27, footwork, `src/mech/dashSteps.ts`):** a side step like rugby's: load (the weight comes off, the hips drop and turn part way toward the dash), the outside leg pushes onto the edge of its foot (its length across the dash) rolling onto the ball and toes while the inside knee lifts, the inside foot plants out toward the dash, the outside leg comes through as the run's first stride (0.58 s; cutbacks 0.71); the body leans into it about the outside hip. **Lean into a change of velocity (Keith 2026-10-31: "lean into it briefly when it changes direction"; Hull topic, group Lean; `WalkerSim.accelLean`):** looks only; the body's velocity (smoothed 0.02 s) kicks a damped spring through its acceleration (`leanPerAccel` 0.14°/(m/s²) held, `leanStiff` 260, `leanDamp` 0.6, capped `leanMax` 10°), the hips and hull tip toward it (hips `leanShift` 0.25 of the way, rolled about the hips' middle, crouch `leanCrouch` 0.1 m at the cap, none in the dash's steps, which take `leanInDash` 0.6 of it on top of their own lean); feet stay planted. Peaks: dash start 9° forward at 0.09 s, dash stop 10° back, dash sideways 10° (body 30° with the steps' own lean), WASD reversal 6°, 90° change 7°, stop 5°, each back under 1° in 0.2-0.5 s; the sprint's turns stay with `turnRoll` (+1°). `tools/lean-probe.ts`. Closer than 35° to the line of travel a burst, past 125° a cutback. The walker moves the body through the steps (the shove from dash.ts only gives the way); a planted leg at full stretch brings the next beat early. **The speed is a burst by time from the press (Keith 2026-10-31: crisp start and stop; `dashBurst`):** up to `dashSpeedOut` 20 m/s in `dashRise` 0.045 s (Keith 2026-10-31: a touch faster; 90% by 0.033 s, was 0.050 / full 0.067), held `dashHold` 0.28 s (Keith 2026-10-31: "a little shorter"; was 0.42), the line bending toward the stick at `dashBend` 12 m/s² while held (about 8° over the hold; 0 for dead straight), whatever the feet are doing (beats 0.02/0.06/0.01/0.1 s; planted feet the body outruns skid after it; no leaving the ground off crests). **The decision at the hold's end (Keith 2026-10-31: "allow more deceleration if the player isn't going to continue it into a sprint"; `dashHoldEnd`, 0.325 s from the press; was 0.15 s held, `dashHoldRun`, gone):** Space still down then (held since the press), the sprint carries on from the peak (up to `dashIntoRun`) as before; let go by then, a tapped dash is a discrete move: a **hard stop** in `dashStop` 0.1 s (was 0.15) straight onto the stick's walk velocity, its way as well as its speed (to a stand with the stick let go), on the run gait with the steps keeping their cadence, then the walk at full grip: never the sprint's let-go grip curve. About 7.5 m (was 11); the motion is the stick's 0.08 s after the hold ends (was 0.29-0.33 s), nothing across the stick from 0.15 s after (`tools/dash-probe.ts`). The stick against the dash during the hold brakes it (`dashBrake`), then the same stop. Damage cut, crowd shove and trample windows run while `inDash`, so they shrink with it. No backpedal by default (`dashPedalSteps` 0: a reverse dash is instant). A dash drops a rail's charge and none builds through it. Dash sliders. **Slopes (Keith 18:28):** uphill the swinging foot heads out early and the thigh rises toward the hull; downhill legs stop at 93% of straight (`legReachMax`), a foot that can't reach hangs and the mech drops or skids the rest. **Leg room on steep climbs (Keith 2026-11-01: the uphill leg folded into itself, the cannon through the shin, and flicked through the ground):** a pose with the knee or hock shut past `legRoomKnee` / `legRoomHock` (35°), its hock pushed off the cannon or a joint in the ground re-aims the thigh to the nearest angle that leaves room, forward first (`legRoomForward` 20°); the thigh never turns faster than `legTurnMax` (2000°/s), so pose changes ease instead of flicking; facing up a climb the body no longer sinks to the back foot (`climbSink` 0, the share of `downhillReach` kept), which was what squashed the front leg. Climbing 20-30° then stopping: knee jumps per 1/120 s frame 1.4 m → 0.53 m, hock never under 32° (was 0°), the cannon never pushed off (was 21-154 frames); `slope-check`. Every planted foot's skid speed (`Foot.slide`) feeds slide dust and furrows. **Walls:** faces over `wallGrade` (42°) take the speed going into them, all from `wallStop` (58°); take-offs upward capped at `launchUpMax` (6 m/s). **Air and landing (Keith 2026-10-26):** one airborne state for every cause (`launch()`: the crane drop, the sprint's leaps, later blasts; the sim takes off by itself when the ground falls away faster than `leaveG` × g at its speed: never at a walk on the lab's dunes, a few hops a minute at a run). Ballistic hips, legs tucking rising and reaching falling with one foot ahead, pitch with the flight path; touchdown plants the feet where they are, the impact is the speed into the ground (so a slip face is gentle), the squat deepens with it (1 m drop 0.4 m, 3 m 0.9, 6 m 1.3), knees fold and the hull pitches, both feet stay down through it; past `landHard` a stagger, past `landHurt` damage; speed the legs can't run on with skids the feet, taken off at the grip for its speed (`landFriction` the least). Sliders in Air and Landing. Digitigrade legs on a pure sim (slopes, Keith 2026-10-24: a squashed planted leg folds its thigh forward and turns its knee out at the hip; slows uphill with the grade; downhill keeps full speed, plants the feet in under the body, drops the hips a little and tips less nose-down; while moving the body leans over its feet, hips about 0.4 m ahead of the planted feet, Keith 2026-10-24): gait phase, duty factor, landing spots predicted on the ground and tilted to slope, mechanical swing and stomp, heel roll-off, body rides the feet and sinks toward the lower one, weight shift and hip roll with a level hull, footfall thud, committed heading, quicker steps near full stretch, settle steps when stopped. Cannon angle measured from the hip-ankle line so the lower leg pitches through the stride, gives under load, opens toward a straighter lower leg at push-off; the heel lifts from 60% of the stance and the foot rolls 48° over the ball of the foot while a hinged toe segment stays flat and pushes, then the toes curl under through the swing and open flat for the stomp (Keith 2026-10-24); since 2026-10-27 the walk pushes off like the sprint (hock opens, heel lifts 70°, cannon folds 45° in the swing) and a swinging toe in the sand bends up (the sprint also eases the foot's tilt, `toeClear`); feet planted further forward; stance comes in from standing to walking width. Walk 6 m/s, Shift runs 8.5 (Keith 2026-10-26, was 4 / 6.5): the stride is capped (`strideMax`, Rampart 7.05 m, Keith 2026-10-27) so faster means quicker steps, not longer reach; past walk speed the hips drop (`runCrouch`) and the bob flips from vaulting over the planted foot to sinking onto it (`runBounce`). Idle flex (Keith 2026-10-26): standing a while with a pilot in, a few slow steps in place now and then, toes curling (group Idle) | `src/mech/walker.ts`, `walkerTuning.ts` | walker |

| Sensor heads | Keith 2026-10-26: a sensor is either a **head** at the front of the hull that follows the aim, or a **cluster** on top. Sensor bases carry `sensor.style` (and a cluster's `sweep` °/s); hulls list their sensor sockets (`mounts.sensor`: Rampart front + top, Siege top only, Salvager front only) and the fit rule matches style to socket. Heads: Rampart visor (the default, 150 m, lock cone +2°), Twin-optic (120 m, +4°, gun tracking +12%); clusters: mast 180 m, survey dish 210 m (sweeps 40°/s), long array 280 m; heads help the aim a little (`sensorLock` widens the soft lock's line-of-fire cone), clusters reach further. Motion is cosmetic and drawing-side (`src/mech/head.ts` HeadDriver: damped spring capped at the turn rate, yaw and pitch limits from the hull, leads a turning torso and looks ahead along a sweeping aim, idle scan); the Rampart drives it each frame (`aimSensor`, called by MechUnit after the pose). Models (`src/mech/sensorModels.ts`): `public/models/sensors/<id>.glb` if there, else code stand-ins (visor head with an orange lens, lamps and armour; twin-optic; mast, dish, array) with the nodes `Head`, `HeadPitch`, `Sweep`. Sliders: "Sensor head" (mech topic in the combat, loadout and convoy labs; hull topic in the walker lab); hold V on the head | `src/mech/head.ts`, `src/mech/sensorModels.ts`, `src/mech/rampart.ts`, `src/items/bases.ts`, `src/items/loadout.ts` | walker (`?sensor=`, `?cam=head`), loadout |
| Mech variants | Seven Rampart variants (Keith 2026-10-24), any lab with `?mech=<id>`: `rampart-stilt` (stilt legs, Suzaku scout), `rampart-turret` (headless M1-style turret, Stratford-Aegis), `rampart-railgun` (railguns on hull arms, Novus-Lux), `rampart-siege` (heavy walker legs, siege cannon, Valhalla), `rampart-salvager` (cab torso, winch and roll cage, Goliath), `rampart-rocket` (compact torso, rocket salvo pods, Akiyama), `rampart-wildcat` (scrap-rebuilt). Same part contract as the Rampart; back kit placed and painted per variant. **Parts mix (2026-10-31):** fitted legs and hulls show their variant's legs or torso on any mech with a loadout (`RampartBody.setLooks`; Stilt, Siege, Salvager so far; `AVAILABLE_LOOKS`), the walk following the legs (`legShape`); `?mech=` shows a whole variant instead. Built from Blender scripts in this repo (kit ported from the old project, `tools/blender/`; `run.mjs` queues Blender two at a time; `export_game.py --model --drop`) by `mech-modeler` agents with the `mech-modeling` skill | `src/mech/variants.ts`, `public/models/mechs/`, `tools/blender/mechs/variants/`, `docs/mechs/` | walker, all labs |
| Upper body | Twin-stick: torso turns toward the mouse aim at a capped rate within a limit from the legs. Each hull gun on its own (Keith 2026-10-24): aims from its own shoulder, swings inward at full speed and outward slower (so the arms turn at different speeds as the aim sweeps), its own spin-up, fire cone and fire clock (small left/right spin and rate splits), recoil; a limited drum per gun (1200 rounds), R resupplies in the lab. Guns dip 20° and raise 50° (`podPitchUp`, Keith 2026-10-26: couldn't reach the tall walkers up close); past that the hull leans back up to 12° (`torsoLeanMax`, `torsoLeanRate`; the Rampart's hull pitches with it), and a target higher still isn't fired on | `src/mech/upper.ts` | walker |
| Minigun FX | Tracers to the ground (spread cone, ray against the height function), muzzle flash and light, hits by surface (Keith 2026-10-24): sand hits feed the dust system (a low layer torn up and pushed downrange, dust thrown up) plus grit; steel gets ricochet sparks, a few back off the face, and dark smoke; steel wreck stand-ins (`src/world/wrecks.ts`) to shoot at, rounds strike the first steel before the sand; a clang for steel hits; casings ejected outboard bouncing on the ground; aim reticle amber when the guns bear, grey while turning | `src/fx/minigun.ts`, `src/world/raycast.ts` | walker |
| Effects budget | Scale step 1 (Keith approved `docs/proposal-scale.md` 2026-10-26): one gate every effect asks first. Your own rounds' hits and anything within `fxNear` (15 m) of your mech always pass at full; hits from others in one 1.5 m cell in a frame merge into one bigger effect (n^0.25 size, n^0.5 puffs); past `fxThinFrom` (30 m) of the camera focus a falling share is kept (30% at 90 m), evenly spread; pressure (dust asked a frame against `fxDustPerFrame` 400, live dust against 85% of the pool) thins harder and trims extras in rank order (plume, cloud, spray, grit, smoke). Dust sources ask in `DustSim` (API unchanged; `hit` takes an optional count), impacts in `MinigunFx`; `bf.fx.own` is the local player's mech. HUD line "effects budget". Under fire (2026-11-05): others' rounds and blasts within `fxUnderFireNear` (6 m) of your mech keep only `fxUnderFireDust` (0.25) / `fxUnderFireBlast` (0.35) of their dust and smoke, sparks full. Sliders Performance and Effects | `src/fx/budget.ts`, `src/fx/dust.ts`, `src/fx/minigun.ts` | combat labs, walker, convoy |
| Shots | Scale steps 3-4: every `Battlefield.fire` is a seeded shot command (src/game/shots.ts), rows in arrays resolved once a frame: direct fire as a sheet with penetration, the rest as rays, both culled through the register; hits, splash, ricochets, chains and misses written as plain-data events, draws handed to the gun effects. Protocols are fields: split (fan), fire rate (copies over the gun's interval), area (splash), ricochet (bounce off the hit's normal, seeded scatter), chain (hop to the nearest enemy not yet hit); generations capped. Protocol items Splitter, Overclock, Wide bore, Ricochet rounds, Arc link carry them (miniguns). `?proto=` in the combat labs; the stress worst case uses the real Protocols | `src/game/shots.ts`, `src/game/battlefield.ts`, `src/items/protocols.ts` | combat labs, stress |
| GPU particles | Scale step 5: sparks, grit, casings, tracers and dust on the GPU (TSL compute rings, spawn rows in a float texture so it runs on WebGPU and WebGL 2), drawn straight off the buffers; the floor from the detail patch's 1 m grid. Dust drawn with the CPU dust's own shader, unsorted, shadows draped on the ground. Pools: dust 32k (CPU 4k), sparks and grit 8k (CPU 600), casings 2k, tracers 1024 (CPU 160). Each look behind a slider (`fxGpuBits`, `fxGpuTracers`, `fxGpuDust`; CPU path kept) | `src/fx/gpuPool.ts`, `gpuGround.ts`, `gpuBits.ts`, `gpuTracers.ts`, `gpuDust.ts`, `dustDraw.ts` | all with the battlefield |
| Unit look | Plain lit paint (dielectric, roughness 0.55) over the model's vertex colours; no outline by default (Keith 2026-10-24); cel bands and the ink outline are still toggles | `src/look/toon.ts` | walker |
| Minigun sound | Ported from the old game, every gun its own voice: its own spin-up (pitched to its spin-up time) and spin-down from its muzzle, its own growl following its own spin and firing (offset and detuned), a spit per round ungated between the guns; dirt hit per round, impact brap gathered every 50 ms, casing tonk on landing; pan by screen position. Starts on the first click or key | `src/audio/` | walker |
| Recorded sounds | The Pixabay sounds (Keith 2026-11-02) cut into game sounds at load: onset found, then start, length, fade, pitch, variant spread, low and high pass, gain and "procedural under" are sliders (Sound samples topic, one group per sound, in every combat lab and the convoy lab); punch matched to the procedural sound it replaces; recut 0.2 s after a slider moves; off, loading or failed: the procedural sound. In: breach cannon, autocannon, scattergun (the flak recording, Keith 2026-11-03), sniper, rifle, SMG, mortar launch, blasts from 2.5 m (large) and 9 m (giant); robots (batch 2): beam charge (first tick of a beam), beam release (in place of the emitter's brap), deaths (walkers full, scrappers higher and quieter, big ones' core blast adds the big death over the shared blast), each through the robot remix (ring mod, resonator, swept band, all sliders). Distant blasts (batch 3): the 'cannon' recording, darkened, heard from 30 m toward the blast so the off-screen ones sound (they used to be silent) | `src/audio/samples.ts`, `sampleCut.ts` (pure), `sampleBank.ts`, `src/game/robotBeam.ts` (robot hooks), `src/expedition/loop.ts` (distant blasts), `public/sounds/pixabay/` | sounds |
| Stand-in walker | Box salvage walker, kept for testing the walk alone (`?model=standin`) | `src/mech/walkerBody.ts` | walker |
| Ground dents | Keith 2026-10-25, after the Snowflow demo: every mark on the ground (footprints and slides on steep faces, wheel and track ruts, dash furrows, round strikes, blast rims, POI ruts) is a brush into a GPU dent texture around the camera focus (toroidal, 2048² over 100 m, RGBA16F ping-pong, one full-screen pass a frame: scroll, relax, splat up to 128 brushes, clamp; channels depression, berm, compression, surface). Brushes are capsules with a flat-floored hollow and a berm ring holding the pushed-out mass (rim noise and berm grain); prints, ruts and furrows "press" (re-stamping never digs deeper), round strikes "add" (a burst churns a patch). Relax per surface (sand soft and ridgy, heals over ~1.5 min; dirt and gravel shallow and lasting; snow deep and slow, switch on with `?surface=snow`), banked into 0.3 s steps (half floats can't hold a slow decay per frame). Hardness: the crater layer's hardness sliders (rock, rocky sand, shallows; its protected zones don't stop prints), else bare rock. Drawn by the **detail patch**: a 25 cm mesh (148k vertices, one draw) over the window, displaced in its vertex shader by the true ground (`GroundWindow`: DuneField.height at every vertex plus the chunks' own grid, refilled on the CPU as rows scroll in and when chunks are rebuilt or patched by craters) plus the dents low-passed to the lattice; per-pixel normals from the dent texture for the detail below 25 cm; colours from the chunks' own colour function; over its last 4 m it eases back to exactly the chunks' triangles; the chunks drop their fragments inside it (`TerrainStreamer.hole`, shadow pass too). Looks only: feet, wheels and AI stand on DuneField.height (`Battlefield.sinkDepth` is the hook for feet sinking into snow later). The old flat decals (`sandMarks.ts`) were deleted 2026-10-26 (Keith: "we don't need to keep the old decals"); footprint size, round dent size and slides on steep sand moved into Ground dents. Sliders: Ground dents, Ground dents: surfaces (Dunes, Hits, Legs, Dust topics) Footprints take each foot's own shape (Keith 2026-11-01): a foot model's sole is baked once at load into a 48² signed-distance tile in a shared atlas (`src/fx/footprint.ts`; 32 tiles, models with the same foot share one), and a shaped brush reads it in place of the capsule (wall `printWall`, berm `printSpread`, contact `printContact`; `printShaped` 0 brings back the oval). Rampart legs bake from `Part_Foot_L/R` when fitted; striders from their pad, big robots from the bucket (three claws), medium robots a peg, crushers a spike hole (`src/combat/robotPrints.ts`, `printRobots` 0/1/2). The detail patch is denser round the focus (`patchFineArea` 32 m at `patchFineSub` 2, 12.5 cm) so prints press the mesh, not just its shading; skids stamp the foot's sole along the slide (Keith 2026-11-02). | `src/fx/dentRule.ts` (pure rules + CPU copy), `src/fx/dents.ts` (pass, `stamp`), `src/fx/groundMarks.ts` (one API for every mark), `src/world/groundWindow.ts`, `src/world/detailPatch.ts`, `src/world/groundColour.ts` | walker, combat labs, convoy, pois |
| Dust | After Keith's reference video (old repo `art-refs/particles/m2-res_1080p.mp4`): one pool of 4096 puffs, two kinds: clumps (dense, darker, thrown low, settling) and cloud (grows 3.5×, rises, drifts on the wind, hangs about 5 s). Sources: stomps (sand shoved out and carried along the walk), toe kicks as feet leave the sand (more when sprinting), minigun rounds into sand (spray streaks flung up and forward that fall back in a third of a second for the punch, thick packets churned up and shoved downrange, plus a plume thrown up and forward that strings into a column, after Keith's plume refs). Dust keeps the push of whatever threw it, then the wind takes over after `windIn`. Ground surfaces (sand, gravel, dirt, concrete) scale dust and clumps and darken them; only sand exists so far (`?surface=` to try the others). Puffs receive the sun's shadows (shadow map, four soft taps per pixel, looked up across and above the puff so the sand under it can't stripe it), plus a march toward the sun over the dunes per puff on the CPU (every 4th frame each, smoothed) for hill shadow that doesn't depend on the shadow map's area, so dust in shade takes a pale sky-lit grey-brown; they don't shadow each other. Drawn as soft sorted sprites with noise-frayed grainy edges, faked round shading (pale on the sun side, browner beneath) and a depth fade into the sand and the mech; a soft shadow under each puff, cast down the sun's ray onto the dune. Sliders: Dust topic (Dust, Dust: look, Dust: sources, Dust: surfaces) | `src/fx/dust.ts` (sim), `src/fx/dustDraw.ts` | walker |
| Checks | Baked bandit (feet on the ground every frame, no jumps or loop seams, rifle holds shape, playback); soft lock and the register (`targeting-check`: queries against brute force on random scenes, the lock against the old list scan, stable ids, reused slots); convoy (`convoy-check`); death physics (floor matches the dunes, a thrown ragdoll lands that way in one piece, the cap, clearing); robot swarm (on ground, crowding, latching, firing from all three sizes, saw strikes, a few big ones, kills with each size much tougher than the last, stomps and shake-off, small legs in reach, medium and big feet planted on the sand and in reach, walkers stepping and not sagging, no NaN, arm and bucket hits, the elbow above the chassis); robot ragdolls of all three sizes in one piece; mech wrecks (`mechwreck-check`: down or flipped, in one piece, out of the sand, thuds, settles, holds its pose against limp); war band (seeded: no NaN, no vehicle flipped off the ground, rounds fired, gun runs, circling in band, never into the mech, wrecks settle, the next band comes); vehicles on dunes and flat; the bandit swarm; slopes on tilted planes (the uphill thigh folds, slower uphill, full speed downhill with the feet under the hips; `tools/slope-check.ts`); a close target under the guns' depression still fired on; walker on dunes with stand-in and Rampart leg proportions (reach, clearance, sliding, both feet up, knees, knee straight at push-off; reports thigh-back and knee angles at push-off); guns (independent on target, drums, arcs); terrain (determinism, slopes up to 75°, under 12% steeper than 30°). | Red-hot sand | Keith 2026-11-01 (a friend's idea): beams leave glowing trails of red-hot sand that cool over seconds; the hotter the weapon, the hotter the sand and the longer it glows; fused sand stays dark for a while; explosions barely warm. A CPU temperature grid round the focus (toroidal, 256² over 104 m, only warm cells touched; charred-but-cold cells fade at 4 Hz), uploaded as an RGBA8 texture only when it changed; the detail patch turns it into a black-body glow (emissive, ember flicker) and darkens fused sand. Painted by: the walker beam (release line, then each sweep tick drags the trail from the last strike, dwell-heated), strider beam, weapon-quad lasers and rails (tick 0), the player's rail strike (× charge), ignited rounds, blasts (560°: a half-second ember). At night the glow lights the ground round it: the glowing cells are gathered into 6 m patches and the brightest 6 ask the night lights for a real held light (colour by heat, priority 0.7 under blasts). Cosmetic: not seeded or networked. Sliders: Light › Hot sand, Hot sand: by weapon, Hot sand: night light | `src/fx/groundHeat.ts` (sim), `groundHeatTex.ts` (texture, glow), `src/world/detailPatch.ts`, `src/game/robotBeam.ts` (`burnBeam`) | robots, convoy, every combat lab |

| Generated parts | Pipeline for generated parts (Keith 2026-10-31, `docs/proposal-partgen.md`): ComfyUI concept image, then lane A (TripoSplat splat, meshed here: opacity field from the Gaussians, surface nets, colour from the Gaussians, floaters dropped) or lane B (Pixal3D / TRELLIS.2 textured GLB, texture sampled into vertex colours); prep orients, fits to the kind's box measured from the game's models (hull = the Rampart's `Part_Torso`, hull weapon = its right minigun pod, shoulder = the W1 shoulder launcher's `Body`, wreck 7×3.5×5 m), simplifies with meshoptimizer to the asset contract's budgets at three levels (`docs/asset-list.md`: hull 6,500 / 2,200 / 700, hull weapon 5,000 / 1,700 / 550, shoulder 2,000 / 700 / 250, wreck as a big POI piece 25,000 / 8,000 / 2,500) into `<id>.glb`, `-lod1`, `-lod2`, the contract's socket names (`HullMount_L/R`, `Sensor_Front/Top`, `Pack`, `Waist_L/R`; `Mount`, `Muzzle_0`), flips inside-out meshes; GLB + sidecar + `index.json`. Two samples from made-up splats. Not yet run against the real models (needs Keith's PC) | `tools/partgen/`, `public/models/gen/` | parts |
| Weapon models | The library every weapon draws from (`src/weapons/library.ts`): a model by id (`createWeapon(id, look)`, after `preloadWeapons()`), loaded from `public/models/weapons/<id>.glb` + json sidecar when the file exists, else a `-left` mirror of the right one, else a code stand-in, so the desktop Blender agent's models (`docs/asset-list.md`) drop in with no code change. The desktop agent's W1 rocket launchers and rocket as delivered. Converted from the old kit: minigun L/M/H, hand cannon L/M/H, missile pod L/M/H (+ left files), back drums, belt segments. Code stand-ins built with a TypeScript port of the old Blender kit (`src/weapons/kit/`: lofts, lathes, sweeps, ribbed hoses, bolts, hazard stripes, chevrons, stencil digits, louvres, lamps): heavy mortar, line mortar rack (seven tubes), rail cannon hull and two-handed, autocannon, cannon, plasma cannon, laser projector, and the rocket, missile and mortar-shell projectiles. A `WeaponModel` has sockets (`Muzzle_n`, `Mount`, `Grip_R/L`, `Eject`, `Laser`, `Glow_n`, `FeedIn`), nested moving parts (`Spin`, `Slide`, `Barrel`, `Recoil`, `Lid`, `Door_n`, `Missile_n`, `Tube_n`, `Launcher`) and setters (spin, doors, missiles, slide, elevation, tube recoil, lid, kick, heat, lamps, paint scheme). Look (`weaponLook.ts`): paint by section from schemes (default the Rampart's khaki and olive), wear, sand dust on top, glinting bare metal, glowing lamps, barrel heat glow, rim light, a 1.1 px weapon-only ink line; a faint red targeting laser (`laserSight.ts`) on the minigun, launchers and pods. Fit on the Rampart (`mounts.ts`, no change to `rampart.ts`): hull guns on the gun pivots in place of the stock pods, shoulder weapons on the hull top, back weapons over the drums; scale 1.0. The arsenal draws every weapon and projectile through it (`models.ts` -> `modelSwap.ts`, `projectileDraw.ts`) | `src/weapons/library.ts`, `catalog.ts`, `parts.ts`, `weaponModel.ts`, `weaponLook.ts`, `palette.ts`, `laserSight.ts`, `mounts.ts`, `demo.ts`, `kit/`, `build/` | armoury |
| Assets | Rampart, back drums, belt link converted from the old repo's FBX; the bandit (Mixamo Vanguard + six clips baked to an animation texture by `tools/mixamo/bake-bandit.ts`); provenance in `docs/assets.md`; converter `tools/fbx-to-glb.ts` | `public/models/` | |
| Battlefield | The shared combat stage (`src/game/battlefield.ts`): dust, ground dents, gun effects and sound, wrecks, and every target; `fire()` resolves a round against the nearest unit of another team, steel or sand, draws it and applies damage; **sheet hits** (Keith 2026-10-26, `src/game/sheet.ts`, `sheetFire.ts`): the player's direct fire (minigun rounds, the rail's slug, autocannon shells per step) is a vertical sheet along its line on the ground: anything whose hit circle comes within the sheet's half-width of the line and reaches into the band from under the ground (`sheetUnder` 0.6 m) to the top (the floor from the ground under the muzzle to the ground under the aim, plus the sheet height) is hit; robots by the part the line crosses (the body when it crosses the body, else the nearest leg); targets without a sheet of their own are found by a fan of 15 rays through the band. **Penetration** (Keith's idea): each shot has a budget in metres of steel equivalent (hull minigun 1, a held light minigun about 0.3 from its pierce stat, autocannon 1.5, rail 10, light rail 6); a body costs its chord × its density (bandits and cutouts 0.45/m, scrappers 0.5, cutters 0.8, excavators 1, vehicles, mechs and steel posts 1.5, wrecks 4), sand above the sheet's top 3/m; walked in order along the line, the shot stops inside what it can't pay for; each body takes damage × (budget left / budget)^0.5 (`penCurve`); sparks where it exits. Enemy small arms still stop on the first thing their ray meets; units give a real surface normal so sparks ricochet off vehicles and mechs (`sparkSize` slider). **Soft lock** (`src/game/targeting.ts`, Keith's ray rule): the enemy under the cursor along the camera ray (tall ones by their body, or by the cursor anywhere on the sand among a walker's planted feet: `TargetCandidate.foot`, Keith 2026-10-26), else the one nearest the line of fire through the cursor out to gun range (at least `lockMin` 50 m: a sword's range is only its reach; under the cursor out to `lockFar` 120 m), picked from the **register** (`src/game/register.ts`: every live hittable thing on one 4 m grid, rebuilt each frame from its owners, stable ids; `near`, `nearest`, `ray` with chords, `cone`, `each`; `Battlefield.register`, labs add owners, `CombatLab.enemies` is the filter); aim at centre of mass; thick orange brackets on the lock (pale when the guns don't bear). **Target lock** (Keith 2026-11-01, `src/game/targetLock.ts`, pure): Tab with an enemy under the soft lock holds it: cursor, aim, shots and the sword's close/far cut all act as if the mouse were on it; red brackets, a turning diamond and a ring on the sand; Tab again, its death, going down, getting `lockHold` 160 m off or hopping out lets go (in the convoy lab Tab with nothing to lock still cycles the seeker); a toggle command, ready for a pad or touch button. Sliders in Your mech > Targeting. The player's aim and weapons are shared helpers (`src/game/playerAim.ts`: both mouse buttons, the soft lock with lead, reticle and brackets, camera shake, the arsenal and blast shake) used by `combatLab.ts` and the convoy lab. `src/game/combatLab.ts` is the shell every combat lab shares (terrain, light, the player's Rampart on twin-stick, iso camera, panel with topics and hold-V, Report, hull bar and red edge when hit, demo) | `src/game/` | warband, bandits |
| Striders | Keith 2026-11-01, reshaped the same day after his first look ("long legs, a really tall body, really short arms: ridiculous"): a mech-sized enemy between the Rampart and the tall terraforming walkers, on the mech's own walker rig scaled up. The player's `WalkerSim` runs in a space S times smaller (`striderScale` 1.7; ground height(x·S, z·S)/S), so gait, slopes and the leg solve are the mech's own: hips 6.0 m, disc top 7.9 m, walk 4.8 m/s (3 m/s average in a hunt), 1.2 footfalls/s. **Shape** (third pass, Keith 2026-11-01): long digitigrade legs ending in pegs (no toes; thigh 1.9, shin 1.75, cannon 1.12 before scaling), a thin spine of bare rods through an abdomen ring, the hull a wide disc (radius 1.9 m, 0.5 m thick: a white lens over a dim one, dark rim and ribs; thickened Keith 2026-11-01, "too plate-like") leaning `striderTilt` 12° (was 24°) toward its prey with a slow drift, the shoulders on its rim (4.1 m across), very long arms (6.2 m) with a dark blade down each forearm past the wrist, a blue wrist lamp and three hooked claws; the red seeker eye runs round the disc's rim as it sweeps. Arms: hung forward swinging long, claws flexing, now and then a twitch; reaching ahead with claws open while rushing; spread while charging. **Grab** (`Striders: tearing`): when its prey is within `striderMeleeReach` 7 m (at most every 3.5 s) it stops: arms up and back over the disc, claws spread and trembling, the eye flaring (0.75 s, the tell), then a strike if the prey is still in reach and within ±70° of its front (90 damage, throws the mech 7 m/s through `MechUnit.shoveFrom`), then it holds on wrenching for 1.1 s (45/s while in reach), sparks and torn plate (`RobotTearFx`). After a beam it rushes in (chance 0.5, up to 8 s, at its run) to grab. Walks to `striderRange` 32 m and strafes. **Red seeks:** while it can't see its prey the head sweeps ±55° with a pulsing red eye and laser; seen 1 s, it may fire. **Blue hurts** (Keith liked these): a beam every ~9 s at 14-80 m: 2.2 s charge (a glow under the head, a blue line on the sand widening to 1.8 m at the lock; the aim follows for 60%), then it ploughs 14 m past the aim (160 damage, pierce 0.8, through `fireRobotBeam`). **Slam** when the prey is within 14 m (at most every 12 s): it stops, a blue column at its feet, an 11 m ring filling for 2.4 s, then `bf.blast` (180 damage, pierce 0.6, a crater), 1.2 s standing after. 4000 hp, armour 8; hits by part (body and head full, arms and legs 0.5, feet 0.3; blasts 0.6 to the nearest part; a round's splash none). Killed: a Rapier ragdoll (chest, head, arms, legs). Cables, pipes and arm hoses dangle on springs. **Hits** (Keith 2026-11-01, "can't hit it with the miniguns"): direct-fire sheets test it as the robots do (`StriderTarget.sheet`): its body when the line crosses the disc's footprint (1.9 m round), else the nearest limb bone; the soft lock aims at the disc. **In the expedition** (`src/expedition/striderCamps.ts`, Striders: in the field): a robot camp brings one per `striderCampEvery` 18 robots, a pack or raid one per `striderPackEvery` 25, at most `striderCampMax` 2; they sleep and wake with their group (a hit wakes it), fight on near the mech once its robots are gone, stream out and come back remembered; `stridersOn` 0 turns them off. No ragdoll in the field (no death world there). | Convoy | A train (Keith 2026-10-24): six platforms in a line along the route (the 40×16 m mech bay leading, a 30×14 m tracked workshop, then wheeled housing, motor pool and fuel cars, and a second 40×16 m tracked mech bay at the back with its crane aft, Keith 2026-10-25), 9 m of sand between them for the mech to walk through (`trainGap`, was 12; bridges 11 m); decks are cut-cornered octagons, not rectangles (Keith 2026-10-25: more hexagonal; `outline.ts`: bigger cuts at the front, small at the back, a pointed prow on the lead), and the walkable deck, push-out footprint, rails, seams and edge pipes follow the outline; each car's middle on its line's path and turned along the chord between bogie points 40% of its length either side, so the line snakes through turns; in a turn the following cars drop back so the inside corners keep the gap (`spread`); optional two shorter lines side by side (`trainLanes`). Footbridges (`bridgeCount` per gap) between landing towers 3 m above the decks (the mech walks under): two telescoping gangway halves drawn between the cars' landings each frame, lifted clear like drawbridges when a tight turn pulls them out of reach. Dune-style crawler tracks on the bay and workshop: trapezoid pods (long run on the sand, shorter top, sloping ends; `trackPods` per side, standing `trackOut` past the deck so they read from above; the footprint grows with them), tread plates that scroll with the distance travelled. Every track pod and wheel leaves ruts (`GroundMarks.rut`, one per line every `convoyRutEvery` m) and throws dust (`DustSim.wheel`, surface-scaled, drifting on the wind). Seeded pipe runs on every platform (`pipes.ts`, after the old project's greebler: bundles with brackets, flanges and valves along the skirts and deck edges, risers from junction boxes up hut and machine walls and over the top, low or up-and-over runs between machines, none across the bay's walkable deck; `pipeDensity`, `pipeSeed`, `pipeScale`). A technical and a gun truck tied down on the motor pool; escorts (`escortCount` 12, was 4, Keith 2026-10-25: more; technicals, gun trucks, six-wheelers, war rigs and a tank on the war band's car sim in the convoy's teal-grey) ride the dunes either side of the narrow road (Keith 2026-10-26: "really nice having those vehicles meander over rougher terrain"): stations 25 m out, each its own share of `escortSpread` in or out, weaving on its own period (two scouts 30 m ahead of the plough's blade, 24 m out, two trailing the tail); each picks a lane over the dunes ahead every 0.5 s and a line from a fan of headings every 0.2 s by the slope along and across the way (climbs under `escortSteep` 26°, side slopes under `escortRoll` 18°, a face taken straight down rather than across), backs out and goes round when stuck (further on each try), eases off over rough ground; the cars and plough are solid to them (shoved out; nosed in at a car, it backs out; a mech blocking it by the cars, it waits then backs off; squeezed across the train, it takes that side; Keith 2026-11-06); the convoy's own trucks carry a dune kit (climb +6°, longer travel); each surging faster and slower on its own rhythm (`escortSurge`), match speed, keep clear of platforms (a hard rule over everything else), each other and the mech (round it on the outside); their guns fire from station (Convoy defence); `escort.mode` is still the hook for breaking station to chase (not built). The convoy rolls along a route at 2.2 m/s (Keith 2026-11-06: two thirds of the 3.3 it was; 2026-10-25: 4 → 3.3) on a heading slider with slow wander; the plough at the front cuts a road about the train's width (11 m half-width, edge blend, berms of the sand it moved; see Plough and wake) into the dunes through an optional `DuneField.modifier` (still a pure function of position and the convoy clock) that stays behind as a road home; chunks behind the blade are patched in place. A jib crane on the bay (slew, luff and hoist each with a top speed and acceleration, quick since Keith 2026-10-25: pick-up about 12.7 s from coming in range to standing on the deck, launch about 10; the crane tracks a moving target, so a mech standing still ahead of the bay is picked up as the convoy rolls past, Keith 2026-11-06.4 s from the pad to letting go; the cradle hangs on the rope as a damped 2D pendulum pushed by the jib's and the platform's accelerations, the hanging mech leaning with it) picks the mech up automatically once it stays in range for 0.4 s (leave range before the cradle grips and it doesn't), lifts it over the rail, swings it aboard, holds it over the set-down pad until the swing settles and sets it down. Then the mech walks the moving deck (Keith 2026-10-24): the deck is its ground (`DeckGround`), the platform's motion carries it each frame (`WalkerSim.carry`), rails and deck furniture keep it on. Two parking bays: E kneels the mech there (Space dashes while walking free on the sand or a deck, as in the combat labs) ("the pilot can climb out here", not built), E stands it up. Defences (Keith 2026-10-25, `defence.ts`, `defenceBody.ts`): two twin-gun turrets per car on sponsons at the cut corners (`defenceCount`) and a heavy one on each mech bay's cab roof, slewing at their turn rates toward the nearest threat in range, sweeping about their rest bearing when idle; they fire since 2026-11-01 (see Convoy defence; `?threat` puts a marker circling the train for them to shoot at). With two bays the nearer crane comes for the mech, which then walks that bay's deck. Two launch pads by each crane: stand on one and the crane comes, lifts the mech out over that side, steadies it with the feet `craneDropHeight` (3 m) over the sand and lets go 13 m out from the middle, on the road's shoulder (`craneDeployReach`, was 21 on the old wide road) (Keith 2026-10-25): the walker's own air state takes it from there (2026-10-26): it flies keeping the convoy's velocity, legs reaching for the ground, and lands with the general landing (a 3 m drop hits at 7.8 m/s: a 0.8 m squat, knees folding, hull pitching forward, back up with a little overshoot; the mech's own landing throws the dust, prints and bang; the lab adds a burst between the feet, a crater mark and a camera shake), forced to follow for 2 s before WASD returns; step off first and it cancels; **Walk off (Keith 2026-10-31):** no rails along a mech bay's long sides (a low kerb and hazard marks; the ends and corner cuts keep theirs); the mech walks, runs, dashes or sprints off and flies with its own speed plus the deck's (pushing off at least `deckStepOff` 2 m/s and always enough to land clear of the tracks), landing as from a crane drop (falling > landing, no forced follow); the expedition starts the same way and the crane re-arms only once out of its range; the pilot keeps an invisible edge (`walkRailIn`); `deckOpenSides` 0 brings the old rails back (`deck.ts` `offSide`, `carry.ts` `walkOff`; Walk off group in the Deck topic). Walking back up onto the deck isn't possible: the crane picks up as before. G launches from anywhere on the deck. No sand prints or dust on the deck. | Expedition loop | Keith's core loop (`docs/proposal-expedition.md`), in the convoy lab. **States** (`expedition.ts`, pure): aboard → launched (the crane drops the mech on the sand) → out → gripped by the crane = banked → aboard; hold Q 1.5 s (`recallHold`) or run out of fuel = forced recovery, a destroyed mech = lost (wreck shown 2.5 s): the at-risk list is lost, never the fitted gear or the bank; recovery is instant (a 1.2 s fade, back on the lead bay's pad, repaired and refuelled). **At risk**: scrap (and items, nothing drops them yet) picked up since the launch, merged per source; scrap is the one currency; the convoy's bank. **Fuel** (`fuel.ts`, in seconds of walking): 14 min pack; standing 0.08×, walking 1×, sprint 2.5× (54% more per metre), dash 4 s each, boost +5×, stripping +0.5×; burns only out; amber under home × 1.3 + 60 s, red under home + 20 s (a two-tone beep). **Return point** (`returnPoint.ts`): where a mech walking straight at walk pace meets either crane on the convoy's planned route (`ConvoyRoute.ahead` forecasts past the laid road with the same steering; exact once laid), inside 0.8 × pick-up range; "Home" = that time + 15 s; recomputed at 10 Hz. **Radar** (`radar.ts` pure, `radarDraw.ts` canvas, `radarSound.ts`): **enemies only inside the mech's sensor range** (Keith 2026-11-14: mechRadar from the fitted sensor × `radarEnemySense` 1, a faint dotted ring on the convoy scope; camps, packs, raids and robots past it don't show, no rim glow; `radarAllEnemies` 1 is the old picture below), POIs steady with a dark edge (tier 1 small, 2-4 gold, fortress and hub a square with its name), heat and glows toned down (`radarHeatAlpha` 0.45, `radarBloom` 0.3). Before: convoy radar 1.5 km within the 450 m range edge (or aboard): POIs as amber diamonds by tier (stripped hollow), swarm camps as heat blobs (size and heat from the camp's guess, off by up to ±40%), unknown areas as static (hide POIs and camps), the route ahead dashed, the cars, the range edge, the mech; past the edge it zooms (1 s, log-eased, a chirp) to the 150 m mech radar: robots as red dots painted by the 2.4 s sweep, POIs, the arrow home on the rim with its distance (pulsing amber or red). Up on the scope = up on the screen. **The field**: POIs from the POI placement round the route (`sites.ts`; not where the road runs; stripped spots remembered), swarm camps on 650 m cells (half hold one, 8-36 robots, leaning small; none within 350 m of the start) and unknown areas on 1.1 km cells (30%, 180-420 m) (`camps.ts`); camps are the robot swarm (`swarmCamps.ts`: one `RobotSwarm` with a `frozen` mask): asleep where they stand, wake within 130 m, on salvage noise or when shot, give up past 320 m or when the mech is aboard, stream in at 650 m and out at 950 m keeping their kills; turrets track awake robots near the train. **HUD** (`expeditionHud.ts`): scope bottom left, beside it OUT m:ss, Home and Fuel with a bar, the at-risk list in amber, banked with a lock, the convoy's scrap, the recall hold bar; salvage prompt and end-of-run notes top centre; the fade. **Packs and raids** (`threats.ts` pure, glue in `loop.ts`, `swarmCamps.addGroup`; Keith 2026-10-26: "a bit slow... more enemies and more excitement"): roaming packs (3 kept 260-520 m round the mech, 6-16 robots) wander and hunt the mech within 200 m (×1.8 while it's loud: firing, stripping, sprinting, dashing), coming in as awake robots within 130 m; a scout pack turns up by 40 s after the drop if nothing has come; raids every ~120 s ±35% (first at 90 s), 26 robots with 30% cutters and 8% excavators from 340 m, homing on the mech's last noise, announced (note, alarm); distant blasts every ~45 s at 140-380 m (dust, smoke, far bang); dust plumes over moving packs, marks at the screen's edge (DUST, DUST · COMING, CONTACT, RAID, BLAST, with distance, until the thing is on screen; `threatMarks.ts`); heat on both scopes (`RadarWorld.heat`; the mech scope shows heat out to 3× its range on its rim). Headless: first fight at 41-55 s, about one a minute after. Denser camps (520 m cells, 60%, none within 260 m of the start) and POIs on expeditions (330 m cells, 85%). The convoy lab has the combat labs' targeting and weapons (soft lock, lead, brackets, per-side buttons, keys 1-3, the arsenal and its HUD line, camera shake; only while driving the mech on the sand); a fresh convoy bay fits miniguns + missile pod (1) + big mortar (2), `?left= ?right= ?k1= ?k2= ?k3=` fit those instead. Packs and raids home on the pilot when he's out on foot. Demo: launches, strips two spots of the nearest POI ahead, follows the return point home. Sliders: Expedition topic (Expedition, Fuel, Way home, Radar), POIs and salvage, Packs and raids (Roaming packs, Raids, Horizon), Swarms | `src/expedition/` (expedition, fuel, returnPoint, radar, camps; glue: loop, sites, swarmCamps; drawing: radarDraw, radarSound, expeditionHud), `ConvoyRoute.ahead` | convoy |
| Convoy | A train (Keith 2026-10-24): six platforms in a line along the route (the 40×16 m mech bay leading, a 30×14 m tracked workshop, then wheeled housing, motor pool and fuel cars, and a second 40×16 m tracked mech bay at the back with its crane aft, Keith 2026-10-25), 9 m of sand between them for the mech to walk through (`trainGap`, was 12; bridges 11 m); decks are cut-cornered octagons, not rectangles (Keith 2026-10-25: more hexagonal; `outline.ts`: bigger cuts at the front, small at the back, a pointed prow on the lead), and the walkable deck, push-out footprint, rails, seams and edge pipes follow the outline; each car's middle on its line's path and turned along the chord between bogie points 40% of its length either side, so the line snakes through turns; in a turn the following cars drop back so the inside corners keep the gap (`spread`); optional two shorter lines side by side (`trainLanes`). Footbridges (`bridgeCount` per gap) between landing towers 3 m above the decks (the mech walks under): two telescoping gangway halves drawn between the cars' landings each frame, lifted clear like drawbridges when a tight turn pulls them out of reach. Dune-style crawler tracks on the bay and workshop: trapezoid pods (long run on the sand, shorter top, sloping ends; `trackPods` per side, standing `trackOut` past the deck so they read from above; the footprint grows with them), tread plates that scroll with the distance travelled. Every track pod and wheel leaves ruts (`SandMarks.rut`, one per line every `convoyRutEvery` m) and throws dust (`DustSim.wheel`, surface-scaled, drifting on the wind). Seeded pipe runs on every platform (`pipes.ts`, after the old project's greebler: bundles with brackets, flanges and valves along the skirts and deck edges, risers from junction boxes up hut and machine walls and over the top, low or up-and-over runs between machines, none across the bay's walkable deck; `pipeDensity`, `pipeSeed`, `pipeScale`). A technical and a gun truck tied down on the motor pool; escorts (`escortCount` 12, was 4, Keith 2026-10-25: more; technicals, gun trucks, six-wheelers, war rigs and a tank on the war band's car sim in the convoy's teal-grey) hold stations either side of the train with a weave (two scout ahead of the lead and two trail the tail, closer in), each surging faster and slower on its own rhythm (`escortSurge`), match speed, keep clear of platforms, each other and the mech, steer round steep dunes; their guns fire from station (Convoy defence); `escort.mode` is still the hook for breaking station to chase (not built). The convoy rolls along a route at 2.2 m/s (Keith 2026-11-06: two thirds of the 3.3 it was; 2026-10-25: 4 → 3.3) on a heading slider with slow wander; the route flattens a 30 m road (edge blend, berms) into the dunes through an optional `DuneField.modifier` (still a pure function of position) that stays behind as a road home; chunks under new road are rebuilt in place. A jib crane on the bay (slew, luff and hoist each with a top speed and acceleration, quick since Keith 2026-10-25: pick-up about 12.7 s from coming in range to standing on the deck, launch about 10.4 s from the pad to letting go; the cradle hangs on the rope as a damped 2D pendulum pushed by the jib's and the platform's accelerations, the hanging mech leaning with it) picks the mech up automatically once it stays in range for 0.4 s (leave range before the cradle grips and it doesn't), lifts it over the rail, swings it aboard, holds it over the set-down pad until the swing settles and sets it down. Then the mech walks the moving deck (Keith 2026-10-24): the deck is its ground (`DeckGround`), the platform's motion carries it each frame (`WalkerSim.carry`), rails and deck furniture keep it on. Two parking bays: E kneels the mech there (Space dashes while walking free on the sand or a deck, as in the combat labs) ("the pilot can climb out here", not built), E stands it up. Defences (Keith 2026-10-25, `defence.ts`, `defenceBody.ts`): two twin-gun turrets per car on sponsons at the cut corners (`defenceCount`) and a heavy one on each mech bay's cab roof, slewing at their turn rates toward the nearest threat in range, sweeping about their rest bearing when idle; they fire since 2026-11-01 (see Convoy defence; `?threat` puts a marker circling the train for them to shoot at). With two bays the nearer crane comes for the mech, which then walks that bay's deck. Two launch pads by each crane: stand on one and the crane comes, lifts the mech out over that side, steadies it with the feet `craneDropHeight` (3 m) over the sand and lets go 13 m out from the middle, on the road's shoulder (`craneDeployReach`, was 21 on the old wide road) (Keith 2026-10-25): the walker's own air state takes it from there (2026-10-26): it flies keeping the convoy's velocity, legs reaching for the ground, and lands with the general landing (a 3 m drop hits at 7.8 m/s: a 0.8 m squat, knees folding, hull pitching forward, back up with a little overshoot; the mech's own landing throws the dust, prints and bang; the lab adds a burst between the feet, a crater mark and a camera shake), forced to follow for 2 s before WASD returns; step off first and it cancels; **Walk off (Keith 2026-10-31):** no rails along a mech bay's long sides (a low kerb and hazard marks; the ends and corner cuts keep theirs); the mech walks, runs, dashes or sprints off and flies with its own speed plus the deck's (pushing off at least `deckStepOff` 2 m/s and always enough to land clear of the tracks), landing as from a crane drop (falling > landing, no forced follow); the expedition starts the same way and the crane re-arms only once out of its range; the pilot keeps an invisible edge (`walkRailIn`); `deckOpenSides` 0 brings the old rails back (`deck.ts` `offSide`, `carry.ts` `walkOff`; Walk off group in the Deck topic). Walking back up onto the deck isn't possible: the crane picks up as before. G launches from anywhere on the deck. No sand prints or dust on the deck. Stand-in looks | `src/convoy/` (route, platform, outline, train, tracks, trail, pipes, furniture, escort, defence, crane, carry, deck, hold; drawing: platformBody, trackBody, pipesDraw, bridgeBody, craneBody, escortBody, defenceBody, kit), `labs/convoy/` | convoy |

| Loot | Keith 2026-10-28 ("scrap ticking up... almost like money", the banking moment, the at-risk list visibly empty; then item levels, power and DPS, Diablo-style drop sounds). **Scrap like money**: a stripped spot bursts into clumps (one per 3 scrap, up to 16, launched over 0.45 s, 0.65 s flights, 5 m arcs) that home on the mech or the pilot; the at-risk count is a rolling number held back by the scrap in the air, so it ticks up as each clump lands, with a steel clank (inharmonic plate partials, a thud, grit) that gets heavier and denser through a streak (0.4 s gap; more layers, the odd chain rattle or ratchet, not pitch) and a dumped-scrap slam on a big haul (40+). **Item drops** (`drops.ts`, pure, seeded from the world seed and the spot id or the robot slot.generation, events): a stripped spot drops an item 30%, small / medium / big robots 3 / 15 / 60%; rarity weights × e^(tier × (0.5 × danger + 0.4 × heat)); level 1 + live depth / 150 m ± 1.5 (cap 60; 100 m until 2026-10-31); protocols at the loot rolls' share. A drop is a crate with a beam in its rarity colour (7 m, +25% per tier), a halo and a ground ring; a Legendary's beam flares 3× for 1.4 s. Taken with E, not walked over (Keith 2026-11-06): E takes the nearest drop in reach (mech 4.5 m, pilot 1.8 m) when it's nearer than the salvage spot in reach (the press is spent on the drop: no strip until E is let go); `autoPickup` 1 is the old walk-over. Each drop within 45 m (10 nearest) has a label (`src/expedition/dropLabels.ts`; group Loot labels): name in its rarity's colour, level, power, DPS for weapons, ▲/▼ against the weakest fitted item of its kind; the one E would take gets a frame and [E] TAKE (or CARGO FULL). Left 400 m behind a drop vanishes. **Items**: `level` (old saves level 1; each level moves a stat toward better by 4% × its weight in `src/items/level.ts`); `itemPower` (100 × mean of rolled/base, flipped for lower-is-better, with Protocols) and `itemDps` (damage × rate × split × rate Protocols; launchers per volley over cooldown + charge) in `src/items/power.ts`, shown on the bay card and rows, the at-risk lines and a pickup card (name, rarity, level, power, DPS, +/- against the weakest fitted item of that kind). **Loot panel** (`lootHud.ts`, in the expedition HUD's column): AT RISK with the rolling scrap and item lines flashing in; banking (on the crane's grip) counts the scrap into the convoy total over 1.4 s with clanks, thunks each item across as a heavy crate (0.5 s each), an industrial clunk and a power-chord sting, BANKED; a loss strikes every line through and fades it (0.15 s apart, 1.4 s each), LOST and a sludgy falling guitar drone. Sounds procedural (`src/audio/lootSound.ts`, Keith 2026-10-28: Mad Max, not gacha): items are an electric guitar (Karplus-Strong strings, tanh drive, cabinet filters, room): Common a palm-muted chug, Uncommon two chugs, Rare a ringing power chord, Epic a power chord bent up a whole step over a sub hit, Legendary a held low power chord, feedback swelling into a squeal while the chord whammy-dives, a crash and a deep hit; pickups the same, shorter. Sliders: guitar drive, tone, room, volume per tier; scrap clank weight, brightness, streak build, volume. Banked items go into the bay stash (convoy lab). Sliders: "Loot" group (Loot topic, with "Loot rolls"); hold V on a drop. | `src/expedition/drops.ts`, `loot.ts`, `lootHud.ts`, `dropsDraw.ts`, `scrapClumps.ts`, `src/audio/lootSound.ts`, `src/items/level.ts`, `src/items/power.ts`, `src/expedition/expedition.ts` (events) | convoy (`?drop=rarity[,level]`, `?drop=all`, `?items=N`, `?loot=N`) |
| Contracts and high value targets | Keith 2026-10-31 (`docs/proposal-quests.md`, step 1), in the convoy lab, topic "Contracts and HVTs". **Contracts** (`src/quests/contracts.ts`, pure): dispatch offers one over the radio every 100 s while out (first after 8 s), J takes the newest offer (30 s to answer), hold J drops one, 2 active at most; kinds salvage run (strip 60% of a POI's spots), cull (24 scrappers / 6 cutters / 15 bandits in a camp's circle), bounty (kill a lair's HVT), tow (hook a downed Rampart, drag it to the crane), escape pods (3 pods within 40 m, walk over them); bonus scrap scales with metres from the convoy, paid onto the at-risk list (salvage, cull, bounty) or straight into the bank when the crane picks you up carrying the hulk or all the pods; a lost run fails what was carried. **HVTs** (`hvt.ts`, pure): lairs on 35% of swarm and bandit camps past 400 m from the road, seeded names; 20 scrappers / 5 cutters / 12 bandits killed inside 180 m draw it out after a 6 s warning, from 110 m on the far side; past 420 m it goes back to ground (count reset); dead = spent for the run, 3 drops (one epic, two rare). The HVT (`hvtMech.ts`) is a soot-black rival Rampart with the duel lab's brain at Veteran, items at the area's level + 3, hull ×2, fighting within 260 m of its lair. **Tow** (`tow.ts`, pure): a 9 m cable, the hulk slides on the height function, walk at 0.6, no sprint or dash. Drawing (`questDraw.ts`): gold light pillars at targets (red for the HVT), a box-mech hulk and cable, pod capsules with lamps; contract panel at the top of the expedition column (`questHud.ts`), gold diamonds on both radar scopes, edge marks. Check: `tools/quests-check.ts`. | `src/quests/` | convoy (`?quest=`, `?hvt=`) |

| Extraction pads and convoy unlocks | Keith 2026-10-27/29 (`docs/proposal-extraction.md` Answers, `docs/proposal-economy.md`). **Unlocks** (`unlocks.ts`, pure, prices and steps are sliders in "Convoy unlocks"): extraction pickups 250 (pads do nothing until bought), radar range 120/320/700 for ×1.3/1.7/2.2, crane speed 150/400 for ×1.35/1.8 (slew, luff, hoist and their accels, scaled over the slider values), bought with banked scrap in `src/ui/convoyShop.ts` (beside the bay screen at the terminal, B or E; U shows it alone); bank scrap and levels are saved with the bay save (`scrap`, `unlocks`). **Pads** (`pads.ts`, pure): 700 m cells, seeded by the road's depth ring (chance 0 / 0.25 / 0.45 / 0.6 for Near / Out / Deep / Far, so none near the road), fee = 60 + 80 per km from the convoy now, wait = 30 s + 15 s per km (cap 150 s; Keith: longer further out). Hold E 1.2 s on a pad (8 m): carried (at-risk) scrap pays first, the bank tops up at ×1.5 (`padBankTopUp` 0/1, `padBankMarkup`; Keith unsure which, so both are sliders), else refused ("not enough", or "not bought"). The beacon is loud while you wait: every 4 s it wakes camps within 250 m (robots and bandits), turns war band columns within 500 m (via their noise) and packs within 500 m (`ThreatField.pullNear`), and a raid comes at half the wait (`padRaid`). **You can roam and fight while waiting** (Keith 2026-11-01); holding E on the pad again cancels and refunds 50% to where it came from; the mech or pilot destroyed (or recalled) while waiting loses everything and the fee. At the end of the wait the quadcopter **hovers**, drifting on a figure of eight within 14 m of the pad (36 s a loop); get within 7 m of its ground point (a blue ring on the sand, filling green) and stay 4 s (stepping out drains it at half speed; it slows to hold over you); not boarded in 60 s it leaves and the fee is gone. Boarded, it slides over the mech and lowers a cradle (7 s lift, no driving or guns while clamped), banks everything at 60% of the lift (`Expedition.extracted()`: one banked event, a fade, the mech put aboard, EndReason "extracted"). **Drawing** (`padDraw.ts`): slab with an H, 12 landing lights and a beacon mast (dim by day, up with `look.night`, the beacon strobes on a call), box quadcopter that flies in over the last 14 s, hovers about (the boarding ring under it), lowers the cradle, lifts and flies off toward the convoy. **Radar**: pad blips (blue ring) on both scopes, the mech scope with the price. **HUD**: PAD distance, fee, wait and who pays in the column; the prompt at the top. | Depth and salvage heat | Keith 2026-10-28: "the further away from the convoy you are, the more enemies there should be", scrapping should "feel risky", combat harder sooner. **Depth** (`depth.ts`, pure): distance from the convoy's road (laid + `ConvoyRoute.ahead` plan, searched 2.5 km back to 5 km ahead of the head), the same at any clock time; **danger** = ((depth − 100) / 1,400)^0.6: 0 until 100 m, 0.3 at 300 m, 0.7 at 900 m, 1 at 1,500 m, capped 1.5 (front-loaded 2026-10-31 so the first few hundred metres feel it; `depthCurve`); rings Near <400, Out <1,000, Deep <2,000, Far. All thresholds and every multiplier are in the Depth slider group (Expedition topic). **At danger 1:** camp chance ×1.5 (cap 95%), camp size ×2 (camps 6-24 robots, 45% of cells), +40% cutters and +12% excavators in camps and at least floor(2 × danger) excavators (`campBigMin`; an excavator reads as 8 robots on the radar); roaming packs ×2 in number and size (6-16, +30% cutters, +10% excavators, at least floor(1.5 × danger)); raids of 20 ×2.5 as often, ×2.5 bigger (+30%/+12%, at least floor(2 × danger) excavators); bandit camps 3-9 ×3.5 (small groups by the road, warbands of 10-30 deep); war band columns 2-5 vehicles ×2.6, chance ×2.2; enemies take the enemy power curve (below), and once past danger 0.5 the next raid is due within 30 s; scrap per spot ×3. Near the road everything is as before. **Harder sooner:** first fight 40 → 25 s, pack 6-16 → 8-20, first raid 90 → 75 s. **Salvage heat** (`salvage.ts`): rises while stripping (0.025/s × spot value/25 × the stripper's noise), holds 3 s, falls 0.012/s; strip noise radius × (1 + heat) × richer-spot factor, richer spots strip longer; at heat 0.35 the nearest pack is pulled in to hunt for good (`ThreatField.pull`, else one turns up 180 m off), at 0.75 a small raid (10 × the depth raid size) is aimed at you (`heatRaid`), each re-arming below half its threshold; the HUD shows the ring and distance from the road and a heat meter (amber, orange, red) with the thresholds marked; the convoy radar shades the rings round the road. Live threats (packs, raids, the heat answers) measure depth from the convoy as it is now by default (`convoyDepth`, slider "Live depth from convoy" 0-1 blends to the road; camps and scrap always use the road). Lab: `?depth=metres` starts out that far off the road. | POIs and salvage | Placement is a pure function of position (`PoiField`): the world cut into cells (`poiCell` 400 m for tiers 1-2; tier 3 twice that (empty), tiers 4-5 four times: bandit fortresses and manufacturing hubs at tier 5), each cell's hash decides 0-1 POI, its tier (tier weights), kind (weights per biome; `biomeAt` optional, all desert until `biomes.ts` lands), the flattest of `poiTries` spots kept `poiSpacing / 2` inside the cell (so spacing holds and pads never cross cells), yaw and seed; small ones give way to bigger ones nearby. Pads (`PoiPads`, a GroundModifier): a capsule levelled at the mean of the ground it covers, the swell cancelled by a quadratic fit, blended back over `padBlend`; stacked with the convoy road through `ModifierStack` / `addModifier` (`src/world/modifiers.ts`; folds exactly, `terrain.ts` untouched). Tier 1-2 kinds as seeded builders (props as data, sections and spots): rock outcrop with a wedged plate and strut, wreck part (a torn-off mech leg or a curved hull plate with ribs), crashed supply pod (furrow, scorch, chute, crates), debris field (2-3 heaps as sections, 4-8 spots, clutter, on a pad), dead convoy (2-4 burnt raider vehicles via `CarBody.setWrecked`, each a section, ruts, on a capsule pad). Salvage (`SalvageSim`): within `salvageReach` of the hips hold E, a bar fills over the spot's strip time, out of reach it pauses, a spot pays its scrap when done, a section banks when its last spot does; `onNoise` every `noiseEvery` s (`noiseRadius`) for swarms later. Drawn: rings and bobbing markers on spots, billboard progress bars, sparks (`gunFx.sparksAt`), hot bits flung and bouncing, grinding smoke and dust, the grind sound, a pulse on the sand per noise; HUD scrap counter and "Hold E" prompt; overhead map (M, `?view=map`) with tier icons over a few km | `src/world/pois.ts`, `poiPads.ts`, `modifiers.ts`; `src/pois/` (site, kinds, outcrop, wreckPart, supplyPod, debrisField, deadConvoy, salvage; drawing: poiDraw, salvageDraw, salvageHud, mapView) | pois |

Bandits find their way round the shacks on a 2 m flow field (`flowStep`) to gates and to the mech in their yard past 28 m; blocked steps slide or turn round (`infantry.ts`). **Walls are shipping containers** stood on end or stacked two high (55% on end) along each ring's line, bedded in a sand drift; in the ground (`SiegeWalls`, stacked after the pads) the containers' footprint is a sheer 5 m core (faces ≥ 2.3 per m, the walker stops at 1.6) and the drift a gentle berm 2.2 m high over 6 m (≤ 0.54 per m: walked, by bandits too); cover stacks are solid boxes the same way. **Gates** (`gates.ts`, steel slabs): 1500 hp ×(1 + 0.3 per ring in), armour 2, blasts ×1.75 (two base miniguns break them in 6 / 8 / 10 s); at 0 a `gateDown` event, the gap opens in the ground. **Garrison** (`fortresses.ts`, host): outer 30, inner 22, keep 14 bandits (×1.5 health, 2 damage a round), 35% as sentries on the container tops; one alarm wakes every yard; while alert **waves** come, the first with the alarm and then every 20 s (Keith 2026-11-06: "giant waves... swarming out of the gates"): 18 bandits, 8 more for each ring the mech is in past the outer, 300 at most per fortress; half stream out through the nearest two open gates (gaps or blown) in the wall just inward of the mech (one every 0.12 s a gate, from just inside, running out), the rest out of the spawner shacks nearest the mech in its yard (all through the gates once the spawners are down): 3 spawner shacks per yard (door ends to a street, hazard band, lamp), the doors swing open (0.8 s, the lamp flashing) and the bandits run out one every 0.28 s; a spawner is a target (1400 hp, armour 2), shot down its doors blow off and nobody comes out of it again, all down no more waves (`spawners.ts`, `spawnersDraw.ts`); **lookout towers** (`towers.ts`, `towersDraw.ts`): three containers stacked crossed, 7.5 m, near the gates (3 in the outer yard, one less per yard inward, the keep at least 1), a crew of 1 or 2 on top (rifles 70 m, grenades 50 m every 3.2 s, rockets 95 m every 4.5 s, both telegraphed with a landing ring); a tower is a target (1800 hp, armour 2): shot down it topples, its stack leaves the ground and the crew dies with it; yards go round to an open gate and through it after the mech one wall away, else hold posts; nobody on foot climbs the containers (`fence`, rises over 0.8 per m). **Bandit mechs** (`fortMechs.ts`, posts in `fortMechPosts.ts`; Keith 2026-11-06: beatable by the duel's rules, escalating inward): each a field rival (`src/game/fieldRival.ts`: the duel lab's brain on a rolled loadout, items relative to the bay loadout's level): one Recruit in the outer yard (items a level below yours), one in a three-ring fortress's middle yard (your level), and in the keep the **warlord** (Veteran, your level, hull ×1.3 on the Veteran's 1.15, dark red) with two Recruit flankers a level below; each fights within 30 m of its post (the warlord and flankers the keep) once its yard is alert and the mech within 110 m; hitting one alerts its yard; the warlord drops 5 hot items, the rest 1; who died and the health left are kept per fortress. **War band**: one column of 4 out of the fortress 20 s after its first alarm. **Salvage**: 5 spots per yard (70 scrap, more inward), the vault in the keep (900, 8 s) also rolls 4 hot items. **Hubs** (unchanged ring, 3 open gaps): 4 fabricator towers (2500 hp, armour 4) build 4 robots each every 22 s while the hub is awake (within 200 m or hit), 28 alive at most; all towers down: the hub is dead and 4 s later its boss rolls out of the core (a giant excavator: ×2.2 size, ×14 health, ×2.5 damage; stand-in from the robot parts), killed it drops 4 hot items; the core strips in 4 s for 520 scrap and 2 hot items (stripping it first calls the boss too). | Craters | Blasts dent real ground (Keith 2026-10-25): a layer in the height function (`DuneField.dents`, added after the modifiers), so feet, wheels, AI, chunks and the death floor all follow it. A sparse 0.5 m lattice in 16 m tiles holds the sand moved; height = softness × saturated sum × one global slump factor, bilinear between nodes. A crater is a bowl and a rim (rim slider 1 balances the volume), fitted on the lattice: sand slides off steps steeper than the wall limit (30°) or than the ground already was, then a chamfer pass settles the rest; stacked blasts saturate toward a max depth. Hardness: bare rock 0.95, rocky sand 0.6, shallows 0.15; POI pads, the convoy road (any ground modifier's floor) and `protect()` circles fully hard plus a 4 m margin, softening over 4 m more, so they never change height. Craters slump back (95% after 180 s), healed tiles are dropped, past 200 the oldest heal over ~3 s. Hooks: `Battlefield.dent` from shells (`burst`: tank shells, bandit rockets, robot charges) and raider blow-ups; combat labs patch the touched chunk vertices in place (`TerrainStreamer.patchArea`, water sheet too) and rebuild the death floor; `?craters=N` sets off N blasts round the start **Big craters land over ticks (Keith 2026-11-13: sim times over 80 ms near raids):** one over `craterStepNodes` / 4 lattice nodes (12000: from about 8 m of radius; a dying walker's are 16-24 m) is fitted by a generator (`fitSteps`) a fixed amount of work per `update()` (ground reads, tile softness, sweeps), nothing of the layer changing until its last part: it lands 0.3-0.6 s after the blast through `update()`'s `changed`; a blast on top of one waits behind it; a change under it (the plough's cut) starts it over, a third time finishes it at once; counted, not timed, so the sim worker's copy lands it on the same call. 0: all at once; `settle()` for checks. **Open sand told cheaply (2026-11-13):** `GroundModifier.noFloor(x, z, r)` (the road by its grid's segments, POI pads by their cells and buckets, the stack all of them, walls always) lets `protectedAt` skip its 17 modifier reads where no floor is within the margin: the same hardness everywhere (crater-check), 11.7 → 7.4 µs a read within 150 m of the road. | `src/world/craters.ts`, `terrain.ts`, `chunks.ts`, `src/game/battlefield.ts`, `combatLab.ts` | warband, bandits, robots |
| Shoulder gear | **Keith 2026-10-30** (`docs/proposal-shoulders.md`): every hull has two shoulders (keys 1, 2) whatever its hull mounts hold; no arm needed. Rolled by `generate.ts` (rarity widens/lifts the numbers; tags `kinetic`/`hose`/`fire` for feeds, `energy` for power, for the passive tree); unrolled ones run on the "Shoulder gear" sliders (weapons topic). The default mech (and the convoy lab's fresh bay): miniguns on both hull mounts, missile pod on key 1, belt feed on key 2. 0.8/s, 0.5 bar), **Arc caster** (`arc`: lightning to the lock or the enemy nearest the aim within 6 m, 24 m range, 40 dmg then 4 jumps of 10 m keeping 75%; grounds at the aim with nothing there; 4/s, 0.06 bar; let go it drains over `arcSpinDown` 0.6 s (a quick re-press is quicker); a dash empties it; `arcWhineLevel`, `arcGlow`), **Sunder beam** (`sunder`, Keith 2026-11-02: like the walkers' beam: one click commits it, paying 3.5 bars then (not enough: it won't start; only a dune or steel ends it), swinging after the aim at 70°/s; the walk slows 50% while it runs; digs and heats the sand; 3 s cooldown; a dash cuts it off). the blade a sheet swept across the arc, each body once per swing to a cleave budget; blade stall, hit stop, lunge by distance; keyed moves (cut, back-cut, return past the waist) eased from a stop to a stop for weight (a short drive bursting into the cut, a long slowing follow-through), the arm swings it (IK) from a shoulder mounted out from the hull and rests it laid back over the shoulder, elbow low and forward; cuts at near full extension; a swish ribbon and heat-haze along the arc; a double click turns the cut vertical at the last moment (Keith 2026-11-04: overhead down from the forehand, rising up from the backhand; harder, one line, a smaller cleave budget; Weapons › Sword vertical cut); `sword.ts`, `Arsenal.stepSword`, `Rampart.holdPose`). **Round guns** (Keith 2026-11-02, `src/weapons/guns.ts`): the SMG, battle rifle and scattergun (shotgun) in a hand, the longshot (sniper) rifle and flak cannon on the hull; rounds through the minigun's path (sheets, penetration, armour, Protocols, feeds, magazines) with no spin-up (the first round leaves on the click), pellets per shot (scattergun 10, flak 14 × 18, each pellet exploding where it ends: a 2.5 m blast at 0.75 of its damage, taken as a blast, with its own flash, sparks, smoke, wave and pop (Keith 2026-11-03)), own synth sounds, kick and stand-in models; sniper and rifle carry the targeting laser. **Breach cannon** (`cannon-medium`, `src/weapons/cannon.ts`): a hull cannon firing one big shell at a time on the gun clock (first on the click), leading a lock like the autocannon, bursting through `Battlefield.blast` (crater, wave, the shared blast sound scaled by radius); direct damage to what it strikes plus a blast share; it stops and bursts on the first body it meets (Keith 2026-11-02). A very faint glinting **targeting laser** on miniguns and rocket launchers, stronger as the sun goes down. Every explosive goes through `Battlefield.blast` (damage through `src/game/splash.ts` `splashBlast`, as a blast: swarms measure it to each unit's nearest part); shells, missiles and line mortar shells burst on the first body in their path (`firstBody`); a burst more than `blastGroundReach` × radius up digs no crater; its crater is queued and dug within a per-frame budget (merging close ones), the HUD shows the cost. Items: `smg-light`, `rifle-light`, `shotgun-light`, `sniper-medium`, `flak-medium`, `rpg-medium`, `rail-light`, `rail-medium`, `ac-medium`, `sh-rpg`, `sh-missiles`, `sh-mortar`, `sh-linemortar`; loadout slots `shoulderL`, `shoulderR` (hull `mounts.shoulders`). Stand-in models, one function per weapon (`models.ts`), on the pod pivots, shoulder mounts or the back mount top.
