| Robot swarm | Autonomous terraforming machines in three sizes (Keith 2026-10-24); **restyled 2026-11-01** (Keith: sci-fi, not construction) in white plate over dark grey mechanics, red eyes for seeking, blue weapons, cables and hoses dangling with a procedural sway (stand-in geometry built in code, `robotShapes.ts`, shared `ROBOT_PALETTE`; roster of all robot types in `docs/design.md`). Small ones are mites (cylindrical abdomen, little head), mediums long-bodied with a sensor head and a spinning **blue cutter**, big ones **terraforming walkers**: a thorax disc and slung abdomen 15 m up (`robotBigHeight`) on four long legs (short thigh to a knee level with the body, long shin straight down, small clawed feet; `BIG_SPEC`). **Weak points** (Keith 2026-11-06, `robotWeakPoints.ts`, group Robots: big weak points): each leg has its own health (shot through: a stub, a limp, two gone fell it), a reactor orb on top (×2.5 hits; a kill through it goes up at once, standing); the guns aim at the part under the cursor. **Terraform pulse** (big ones, group Robots: terraform pulse): with the mech within 30 m it plants and charges a beam for 2.6 s out from under it to where the mech stood as the charge began (locked; round 2, Keith 2026-11-06; `robotPulseAim` 0 is the old blast under itself), a ring on the sand at the 11 m blast edge and a second growing out to it, then blasts there: 120 damage, a crater, a shove of 16 m/s outward (`MechUnit.shoveFrom`); `onPulse` event, `fireRobotPulse` (`src/game/robotBeam.ts`). **Own blasts** (Keith 2026-11-02, to kite the swarm under walkers): the pulse also hits robots in its reach (`robotPulseOwnDamage` 220, the walker firing spared; `RobotSwarm.hurtOwn`), and a big one's reactor goes up as its wreck tips over (Keith 2026-11-06, `src/combat/reactorFall.ts`; group Robots: big death): when the hull is down to 0.6 of its height, 1.4-2.4 s in, an airburst about 8 m up with a nova (flash, fireball shell, shockwave ring, ground ring; `src/fx/nova.ts`), slowed ×1.7 with the hull shattering into hot plates and long sparks (Keith 2026-11-06 round 2, `src/fx/novaDebris.ts`), the wreck thrown apart (`src/game/reactorWrecks.ts`); only the tall walkers by default (Keith 2026-11-06 round 2): weapon quads' (smaller and sooner, 9 m, 0.4-1.3 s; Robots: weapon quad death) and striders' are off: a 22 m blast, 90 to the mech and anything not a robot, 450 to robots (chains can happen), a 22 m/s shove, a deep extra layer of the shared explosion sound (its sub boom and a slowed blast); `onDeathBlast` event, `fireRobotDeathBlast`. Medium walkers 5 m/s, weapon quads 4.5, big ones still 3; big ones 2600 hp, shots 55 (Keith 2026-11-06: tougher, scarier). The line beam is blue and digs a trench of craters (`robotBeamDig`). (Earlier text below still says excavator, bucket, chassis: the sim is the same.) **Small scrappers** (0.9 m, 8 m/s, 12 hp): a grey hub with a yellow top plate, cutting disc and jaws in front, a scrap hopper; four legs splayed on the diagonals, scuttling any way without turning, run as a pack, leap from 5 m and cling on the mech, tearing at it (sparks, plates flicked off, a grinding screech) until shot, stomped or shaken off; a fifth hang back and shoot. Light: rounds pierce them, splash hurts them fully. **Medium cutters** (15%, 2.2 m body 1.5 m up, about 2.5 m tall, 3.5 m/s, 90 hp): a crab chassis on four planted legs stepping in diagonal pairs (knees up over the body, a ram on each thigh), a circular saw on the right front, a scrap bin on the back; half hold at 9-16 m firing slugs (7 damage, one about every 2 s; Keith 2026-10-29: hit harder) after a 0.65 s tell (lamps flash red, it rears back, the aim fixes where the mech was), half walk right in; any within 4 m of the mech rears back with the saw spinning up and its lamps flashing red (0.7 s), then lunges where the mech was and saws (12 damage, sparks and torn plate), so a dash in time dodges both (Keith 2026-10-24: skill against the swarms). They stop rounds, take 35% of splash, ignore stomps. **Big excavators** (3.5%, about one a pack of 30, 500 hp): a small chassis (cab, engine house, striped counterweight, gun under the front) 10 m up on four legs that are excavator arms: boom up to an elbow about 15 m up, stick down to a bucket planted in the sand, three rams and a hose on each; one leg steps at a time (the bucket curls as it swings; dust on each footfall), swing in from the flank, stop at 18-30 m and, after a tell (chassis rears, gun lamp red), lob volleys of two slow charges (30 damage each at the centre, a volley about every 2.8 s with a 0.9 s tell: a must-answer threat, Keith 2026-10-29) from the chassis through `Battlefield.lob` (a ring on the sand where each lands; flight about 1-2 s from the charge speed, leading the mech's walk by a share, scattered), so they can be dashed out of; cast long shadows. **Hits by part** (Keith 2026-10-26): a round stops on the first part it meets and sparks there; the body (hub, chassis) takes full damage, legs (a big one's boom and stick, a medium one's thigh and shin) `robotLegDamage` 0.5, feet (a big one's bucket, the low quarter of a medium one's shin) `robotFootDamage` 0.3 (group Robots: hit parts, with the leg hit radii). Walker feet drag along the sand rather than fall out of reach (saw lunges). `robotCentre()` gives aim points for all three, `robotFootprint()` a walker's spread of feet, `robotCandidates()` (robotTarget.ts) the soft-lock list. 300 at about 0.6-1.5 ms a step headless **Charging beam** (excavators, Keith 2026-10-31): beside the volley, every 11 s or so at 12-50 m, a 2.2 s charge (a glow under the chassis growing and heating, a warning line on the sand that widens to the beam at the lock), the aim following the mech for 60% of it, then a 1.6 m beam down the line (150 damage, pierce 0.8, through every body of another team on its low part: mech, pilot, bandits), a furrow of red-hot sand that cools; seeded, `onBeam` event, `robotBeam*` sliders (Robots: beam); resolved on the battlefield (`src/game/robotBeam.ts`), drawn by `robotBeamDraw.ts`. | `src/combat/robots.ts`, `robotLegs.ts` (shapes, leg maths), `robotShapes.ts` (stand-in geometry), `robotsDraw.ts`, `robotTarget.ts`, `robotTearFx.ts`, `robotBeamDraw.ts`, `src/game/robotBeam.ts` | robots |

| POIs and salvage | Placement is a pure function of position (`PoiField`): the world cut into cells (`poiCell` 400 m for tiers 1-2; tier 3 twice that (empty), tiers 4-5 four times: bandit fortresses and manufacturing hubs at tier 5), each cell's hash decides 0-1 POI, its tier (tier weights), kind (weights per biome; `biomeAt` optional, all desert until `biomes.ts` lands), the flattest of `poiTries` spots kept `poiSpacing / 2` inside the cell (so spacing holds and pads never cross cells), yaw and seed; small ones give way to bigger ones nearby. Pads (`PoiPads`, a GroundModifier): a capsule levelled at the mean of the ground it covers, the swell cancelled by a quadratic fit, blended back over `padBlend`; stacked with the convoy road through `ModifierStack` / `addModifier` (`src/world/modifiers.ts`; folds exactly, `terrain.ts` untouched). Tier 1-2 kinds as seeded builders (props as data, sections and spots): rock outcrop with a wedged plate and strut, wreck part (a torn-off mech leg or a curved hull plate with ribs), crashed supply pod (furrow, scorch, chute, crates), debris field (2-3 heaps as sections, 4-8 spots, clutter, on a pad), dead convoy (2-4 burnt raider vehicles via `CarBody.setWrecked`, each a section, ruts, on a capsule pad). Salvage (`SalvageSim`): within `salvageReach` of the hips hold E, a bar fills over the spot's strip time, out of reach it pauses, a spot pays its scrap when done, a section banks when its last spot does; `onNoise` every `noiseEvery` s (`noiseRadius`) for swarms later. Drawn: rings and bobbing markers on spots, billboard progress bars, sparks (`gunFx.sparksAt`), hot bits flung and bouncing, grinding smoke and dust, the grind sound, a pulse on the sand per noise; HUD scrap counter and "Hold E" prompt; overhead map (M, `?view=map`) with tier icons over a few km | `src/world/pois.ts`, `poiPads.ts`, `modifiers.ts`; `src/pois/` (site, kinds, outcrop, wreckPart, supplyPod, debrisField, deadConvoy, salvage; drawing: poiDraw, salvageDraw, salvageHud, mapView) | pois |
| Craters | Blasts dent real ground (Keith 2026-10-25): a layer in the height function (`DuneField.dents`, added after the modifiers), so feet, wheels, AI, chunks and the death floor all follow it. A sparse 0.5 m lattice in 16 m tiles holds the sand moved; height = softness × saturated sum × one global slump factor, bilinear between nodes. A crater is a bowl and a rim (rim slider 1 balances the volume), fitted on the lattice: sand slides off steps steeper than the wall limit (30°) or than the ground already was, then a chamfer pass settles the rest; stacked blasts saturate toward a max depth. Hardness: bare rock 0.95, rocky sand 0.6, shallows 0.15; POI pads, the convoy road (any ground modifier's floor) and `protect()` circles fully hard plus a 4 m margin, softening over 4 m more, so they never change height. Craters slump back (95% after 180 s), healed tiles are dropped, past 200 the oldest heal over ~3 s. Hooks: `Battlefield.dent` from shells (`burst`: tank shells, bandit rockets, robot charges) and raider blow-ups; combat labs patch the touched chunk vertices in place (`TerrainStreamer.patchArea`, water sheet too) and rebuild the death floor; `?craters=N` sets off N blasts round the start **Big craters land over ticks (Keith 2026-11-13: sim times over 80 ms near raids):** one over `craterStepNodes` / 4 lattice nodes (12000: from about 8 m of radius; a dying walker's are 16-24 m) is fitted by a generator (`fitSteps`) a fixed amount of work per `update()` (ground reads, tile softness, sweeps), nothing of the layer changing until its last part: it lands 0.3-0.6 s after the blast through `update()`'s `changed`; a blast on top of one waits behind it; a change under it (the plough's cut) starts it over, a third time finishes it at once; counted, not timed, so the sim worker's copy lands it on the same call. 0: all at once; `settle()` for checks. **Open sand told cheaply (2026-11-13):** `GroundModifier.noFloor(x, z, r)` (the road by its grid's segments, POI pads by their cells and buckets, the stack all of them, walls always) lets `protectedAt` skip its 17 modifier reads where no floor is within the margin: the same hardness everywhere (crater-check), 11.7 → 7.4 µs a read within 150 m of the road. | `src/world/craters.ts`, `terrain.ts`, `chunks.ts`, `src/game/battlefield.ts`, `combatLab.ts` | warband, bandits, robots |

| Pilot on foot | `docs/proposal-pilot.md` with Keith's answers (2026-10-26). **Hold F** (0.6 s, a ring fills on the HUD) to hop out: in the field the mech kneels where it stands (hips 1.1 m down, 8° nose down, 0.5 s), at the convoy only parked in a bay (F in a bay parks it first; elsewhere on the deck the HUD says why not); the pilot climbs down beside it in 1.1 s (left side first, else right, behind, ahead, on the level the mech stands on) and the camera glides in to 15 m; hold F within 3 m of the mech to climb back in (1.1 s), the mech stands up (0.6 s), the camera goes back out. F no longer fires (both mouse buttons can be held). **The pilot** (`pilot.ts`, pure): a capsule (1.8 m, radius 0.3), WASD relative to the screen, walk 1.6 m/s, Shift runs 4.5, turns toward where he moves (720°/s), steps up 0.45 m, never steps down more than 0.6 m (the rails: no walking off a deck or bridge), stairs at 0.6× along the slope; 30 health. **Walking surfaces** (`src/world/walkable.ts`): `walkHeight(x, z, fromY)` over the true ground (craters included) and registered surfaces with moving frames; someone on a surface keeps his spot in its frame, so a turning, heaving deck carries him; a solid surface rising past the step is a wall, thin ones (bridges) you walk under. The convoy's (`src/convoy/walkways.ts`): every car's deck inside its rails, furniture as walls (the cab roof walkable), the bay's crane ring, service tower and terminal, the footbridge landings, stairs (`train.ts landingLayout`: a stair down the side of each tail tower, a stair up the back of the cab to the nose landing on its roof; drawn as treads with rails, the mech walks round them) and the footbridges (each a frame stretched between its landings; a lifted one puts the pilot back on the nearer landing). On the sand the cars' footprints shove him out. **Body** (`pilotBody.ts`): Vanguard from the bandits' bake as one instance in a blue suit (sliders), aim idle as the stand pose, walk-and-fire walk and the sprint as run, speed-matched; a pale ring on the ground under him; climbing is the stand pose moved along a path (no climb clip in the bake). **Bay terminal**: a console between the bays' aft ends on each mech bay; E within 1.6 m on foot opens the bay screen (`src/ui/bay.ts`, now in the convoy lab, saved; B stays a shortcut). **Enemies** (`quarry.ts`): the pilot is a Hittable on the player's team (rounds and blasts resolve on him by geometry); each enemy group takes the pilot as its target when he's within 1.6× the mech's distance of its middle (robots, bandits, the war band; robots cling to him low, 0.2-1.3 m, and shake off on a switch); a parked mech gets attacked like a driven one; a sleeping camp wakes for a pilot at half its wake range. **Expedition**: the pilot killed on foot ends the run (`Expedition.pilotKilled`, at-risk loot lost, bank and fitted gear kept, back aboard); the parked mech destroyed while he's out is the usual loss; the crane never comes for an empty mech. **Salvage on foot**: E from where he stands, 2.5 m reach, 0.5× speed, 0.35× noise. Combat labs: hold F too, a killed pilot is back in the mech after the rebuild time. | Sim worker | `docs/proposal-simworker.md` (Keith 2026-11-06). A module worker (`src/sim/worker.ts`, started by `src/sim/host.ts`) runs beside the main thread; on by default in the convoy lab, `?noworker` runs without it. Step 1: no system moved yet. The worker keeps its own copy of the ground (`src/sim/groundMirror.ts`): the main thread records every call that changes the ground (`src/sim/groundEvents.ts` taps route reset/jump/update, wake clear, the blade offset, crater add/blast/invalidate/protect/clear/update, field update, the sites' keepClear/roadGrew/rebuild/skip memo, gates down, and diffs the values) and the worker replays them in order. Every frame `simMirrorSamples` points (16; Performance) are sampled on both threads (round the mech, across the road at the blade, round the latest blasts) and must match exactly; the HUD's `sim worker` line shows worker ms, lag, events and the parity, `window.__simworker` the counts. The sites' ground (pads, walls) is split out as `src/expedition/sitesGround.ts`. Step 2: the terrain chunks' vertices are worked out in the worker (`src/world/chunkFill.ts`, the same maths both sides): chunk builds (`workerBuilds` out at once, 8; Performance) and patches (the plough's strips, crater flushes) are asked of it in order after the ground's events and copied into the chunks when the answers come (`TerrainStreamer.remote`, `takeBuild`, `takePatch`; a frame late); chunks within 1.5 chunks of the focus are still built on the main thread at once when missing; the HUD line adds the chunks out and the copy-in ms. Step 2b: the detail patch's ground window (`src/world/groundWindow.ts`) asks the worker for its fine rows (`GroundWindow.remote`, `takeFine`; `DetailPatch.setFineRemote`): lead and changed rows go there as queued, coarse rows stay here (copied off the chunks); a row still out when it comes into the drawn window is filled here at once; the first fill and `fillNow` stay here. Checks: `tools/groundmirror-check.ts`, `tools/terrainworker-check.ts` Step 3: the escorts drive in the worker beside its own copy of the convoy (`src/sim/escortsRemote.ts`): the recorder takes the convoy's reset/jump/update as the outermost calls (`GroundRecorder.convoy`), and the lab's `EscortGroup` gets `remote`, so its update (dt, the mech, the guardians' spots), reset and planning-memo invalidations (`EscortGroup.invalidate`) go into the same stream as commands (`GroundRecorder.command`); the worker answers each frame with a snapshot of every escort (pose, wheels, distance moved) written into the lab's list (`applyEscorts`), so the bodies, guns, ruts and dust read them a frame late (ruts and dust once a frame from the moved total). The worker's convoy poses are checked against the main thread's every frame (HUD line). Check: `tools/escortworker-check.ts` Step 4 (the robot swarm, `src/sim/robotsRemote.ts`): the expedition's swarm camps' RobotSwarm steps in the worker, on the worker's ground with its own safe zone round the worker's convoy; the lab's swarm is a view: its outside calls (spawn, damage, hits, stomps, tramples, freezes, targets...) act on it at once and go to the worker as commands in the ground's stream (setters that change nothing aren't sent); `update` hands out the events the worker sent back (a frame late) and sends the step, which the worker runs at the frame's end; each answer's snapshot (every typed array and number field of the swarm, found by name) overwrites the view, and the calls made since are replayed on it silently; a death is handed out once (by uid). The wrecks' reactor positions go over each frame. Death physics (2026-11-07, `src/sim/deathsRemote.ts`): the Rapier world of the dead steps in the worker, the lab's DeathPhysics a view written back each frame (poses, live, done, sink, thuds); check `tools/deathworker-check.ts`. The random stream's state comes back with the snapshot, so calls on the view draw what the worker's do (while an answer is late, a spawn's rolls on the view can differ until it comes). Not moved yet: striders, crushers, droids, bandits, the summoner's allies. Stage 7b (2026-11-07): the bandit camps' infantry swarm (with the war bands' riders) steps there too (`src/sim/infantryRemote.ts`, the robots' way: a view on main, calls recorded, shots back a frame late, a snapshot of its block and arrays each frame); fortress garrisons stay on main (their walls are a function the worker doesn't have). | `src/sim/` | `labs/convoy/` (HUD line `sim worker`) |
| Not started | Mech classes, day-night cycle, mech joint and footstep sound, a game page | | |
| Health, repair kits and abilities | Keith 2026-10-31 (`docs/proposal-repair.md`). **Vitals HUD** (`src/ui/vitalsHud.ts`, every combat lab and the convoy lab): an upright hull bar at the left edge beside the energy bars (from 120 px down, `hullBarHeight` 198, Keith 2026-11-01) with the number and armour under it, a pale ghost of the last hits that holds 0.45 s then drains, a red edge flashing by hit size and pulsing below 30% hull, a two-tone warning as it crosses, red arcs round the mech toward where hits came from (0.9 s), and the **action bar** (`src/ui/actionBar.ts`, Keith 2026-11-01) at the bottom centre: a button per shoulder or back key (1-3; overdrive, ignite, discharge, rockets, missiles, mortars), ability (3, 4) and the repair kit (Q, kits left): a dark sweep and the seconds while cooling, an amber bar while its effect lasts, a white flash when ready (`actionSize` 60 px; `Arsenal.keySlots()`); on foot the bar is the pilot's with the mech's hull thin under it; the bar glows while repaired. **Repair kits** (`src/game/repair.ts`, pure; glue `repairUse.ts`): tap Q, 35% of max hull over 2 s, 1 s lockout, carry 3, refused at full hull or on a wreck, lost mid-repair if destroyed, never refill by themselves: bought at a bay terminal (40 scrap, the shop panel's Supplies row), found in salvage (12% per stripped spot, by a hash of the spot), kept when the mech is lost; on foot hold Q 4 s within 4 m of the mech to patch it by hand. Recovery moved to hold X. **Abilities** (`src/abilities/`, pure + zonesDraw + abilityUse glue): a pack's on key 3, a hull's on key 4 (`Base.ability`): Medic pack (new; 3 bars) Repair beacon thrown at the cursor (20 m), 8 m zone, 2%/s for 10 s, cooldown 30 s; Salvager hull Nanite field round you, 6 m, 3%/s for 6 s, cooldown 25 s; Siege hull Field weld, braced 4 s (rooted, 40% less damage), 5%/s self, cooldown 35 s. Zones heal the owner at 60%, allies (mechs, the pilot) at full, at most 2 zones stack per target | `src/ui/vitalsHud.ts`, `src/game/repair.ts`, `src/game/repairUse.ts`, `src/abilities/` | repair, all combat labs, convoy |

## Known gaps

- **Convoy defence step 1** (2026-11-01): the convoy itself can't be hurt (enemies only hunt the player, so they never shoot at the cars); guardians and escorts use the player's minigun sliders and have no loadouts; the threat list takes the first 96 of each kind within 250 m of the middle car, not the nearest; kills by the convoy drop loot like the player's; enemy packs keep hunting a player who stays by the convoy (they hover at the zone's edge and die) rather than giving up. Judged from numbers and a headless run, not played.

- **`deaths-check` is flaky:** now and then a medium robot's ragdoll part ends up under the sand (-0.3 to -0.85 m); it passes on rerun. Seen since 2026-10-26 on a loaded box.

Things that exist in a partial or stand-in form, or that couldn't be done yet. Remove a line when it's fixed.

- **No ammo belt** from each back drum to its gun. The link model is converted (`public/models/ammo-belt-link.glb`);
  the Rampart's pods have no feed-in point yet, so one needs choosing on the model.
- **Power pack is a stand-in** built from boxes in `rampart.ts`; it needs a modelled one in the Rampart's construction.
- **Weapon stand-ins:** the mortars, rail cannons, autocannon, cannon, plasma and laser guns, the missile and the
  mortar shell are code stand-ins until the desktop agent's models land (`docs/asset-list.md`). The converted miniguns
  are over the 6k budget (10.6-14.5k) and the Heavy pod is 8.7k; their rebuilds are W9 and W4. A pod H costs about 60
  draw calls (31 moving parts plus ink); merge parts if it shows in the frame time. The weapon look's sliders live in
  the armoury lab only; the arsenal uses their defaults.
- **Rampart rocket box hidden, not removed:** it's still in `rampart.glb`.
- **Blender sources not in this repo:** the Rampart's scripts (`rampart_v4.py`, `export_game.py`, kits) live in the old
  repo; changes to the model mean going back there and converting again with `tools/fbx-to-glb.ts`.
- **Vite never run in the cloud workspace** (no npm registry there): builds are typechecked and bundled with esbuild
  and screenshotted headless. The first `npm install` on Keith's PC creates the lockfile.
- **Headless shots are WebGL on a software renderer**, so their frame times mean nothing; real numbers come from
  Keith's PC.
- **No ambient occlusion pass**: crevices only get the AO baked into the Rampart's vertex colours. A screen-space AO
  (Three's GTAO node) would ground the mech and the dunes further.
- **Shadows stop at the edge of the shadow area** (it follows the focus and grows with zoom); with a very low sun,
  far dunes don't shadow.
- **The walk may still not match the old lab's feel**: push-off now rolls over hinged toes and the Rampart has a
  shorter stride, but the old lab's swing was blended in joint angles and had AT-ST hip arms; this sim uses a foot
  path and IK. Keith to judge.
- **Legs can't fully handle the tall dunes**: on steep slip faces the Rampart's feet fall out of reach on up to 10% of
  leg-frames (zigzag) and its knee runs straight at push-off; the walker checks were loosened for dunes to allow it.
  A walker that adapts its stride and stance to slope would let them tighten again.
- **Toe split is one hinge per foot** (all toes bend together) and the Rampart's back spur stays rigid on the heel.
- **Only two hit surfaces** (sand, steel) and the steel is box stand-ins; the old game also had rock, concrete, wood
  and flesh, ground marks and gouges.
- **Dust shadows not seen clearly yet** in headless shots (the low dust sits close to its own shadow at a 36° sun);
  `shadowStrength` may need raising. Dust colour is fixed, not driven by the sun or the time of day. Dust in shade
  is darkened from the shadow map but only checked loosely headless.
- **Plume shape only seen from above** in headless shots (it reads as a dense lump at the strike); its column height
  wants judging in motion.
- **Ground dents only exist in the dent window** (100 m around the focus): at the far zoom (80 m) the window's edge
  can show, marks outside it are gone, and walking away and back loses them (POI ruts are re-pressed). Dents on the
  patch edge band ease out. Prints show their hollow under a planted foot (the foot stands on the true ground, a few
  cm above the print's floor); `sinkDepth` is there for sinking feet when snow comes.
- **Detail patch cost not measured on a real GPU**: 148k vertices drawn twice (colour and shadow), and the CPU refills
  about 2 ms per metre the window scrolls (in node here). Spacing and area are sliders.
- **Pilot step 1 is rough:** no climb, crouch or idle-without-rifle clips (he carries the bandits' rifle and climbs as
  a tween in the stand pose); no ladder up the service tower; the pilot can't get onto the convoy from the sand (no
  ramp: only out of a parked mech) and only the deck rails stop him (no railings as colliders, so he can't fall off
  either); the housing canopy posts and workshop gantry posts aren't walls; escorts and raiders don't steer round
  him (raiders do avoid him like the mech: they never drive into their target); salvage spots have no "small"
  kind yet, so on foot reaches the same spots as the mech, just closer and slower; calling the empty mech (another
  key) not built; no sidearm. Judged from still shots headless, not played.
- **Convoy stand-ins**: the crawler pods, pipes, footbridges and landings are boxes and extrusions; escorts shoot from station but never break off to chase (`escort.mode`); the escorts and the motor pool's vehicles
  share one material, so hold-V lights them together. The turrets' corner sponsons aren't in the push-out footprint (a mech hugging a corner can clip one). With two footbridges per gap the outer landings sit near the cut front corners. Heavier escorts (six-wheelers) pass closer to the cars in hard turns (3.2-3.9 m, was 8.5 with only light ones). The rear bay and its crane have no demo of their own (the demo uses the nearer crane, normally the front).
- **Only the main driving values are sliders per kind** in the war band lab (top speed, acceleration, grip, drift,
  steering); the rest of `CAR_PARAMS` (suspension, slopes) stays at the kind presets.
- **A raider can still rarely pass within about 2 m of the mech** (about one band in six in unseeded runs); the
  check runs seeded.
- **Ragdolls are boxes** (the bandit and robot stand-ins' parts as plain boxes; a Vanguard bandit turns into a box ragdoll when it dies); bandit mechs still just go dark
  where they fall (no topple). Rapier in the cloud workspace is a copy from Keith's PC (npm blocked there).
- **No concrete surface for hits yet** (dust plus a few sparks when something concrete exists); the grey wreck
  stand-ins are steel.
- **Baked bandit is rough in places:** no crouch or cover clip (the sim has no cover); downed bandits topple as a frozen pose; the rifle is boxes and the sprint and flinch carry it in the right hand only; a few triangles at the shoulders and elbows pinch at full swing (linear skinning); the run-and-fire clip is rarely chosen (bandits only shoot while standing in the band).
- **Mech joint, servo and stomp sounds** from the old game (`MechServoSynth`, the layered stomp, the thrum) aren't
  ported yet; only the minigun's.

- **POIs are stand-ins and walk-through:** props are boxes, rocks, I-beams and a capsule; the mech walks through them (no
  collision), rounds don't hit them (not steel for `Battlefield` yet), nothing listens to salvage noise yet, and spots
  are stripped whole (no item drops, no carrying value home; Keith: rarities later).
- **POI pads ride the biome-free ground:** pads cancel the swell assuming `floor = swell + lift`; if biomes change how the
  floor is made, `rawGround` in `src/world/poiPads.ts` needs a look.

- **Loadout step 1 is rough:** legs and hulls show their models (2026-10-31; mixed parts can clip or gap where a
  pelvis meets another model's torso), but arms are still stand-in boxes, the pods are always the Rampart's minigun
  whatever the hull, and a hand gun or two-hander draws as the hull minigun pod; a two-hander fires from the
  right pod only. Shared gun sliders tune a gun only while its values match the first gun's (else the item's own
  values win). No bay pad or convoy bay yet (B opens it anywhere); no dragging (Protocols are picked from a list).
  The bay's cargo list rides out at the launch (2026-11-01); the inventory (I) is the field view of the cargo.

- **Craters are rough in places:** a crater's bowl is only a few chunk vertices across at the 1 m grid, so small
  ones read soft; the convoy lab doesn't wire craters yet (the road is protected through the field's modifier, but
  `craters.invalidate` should follow `route.onChange` when it does); the wall limit is fitted on a 0.5 m lattice, so on
  sharp dune creases a few points run 2-4° over; dense tiles never shrink until they heal.

- **Packs and raids are rough:** raids are robot waves only (no vehicle war band yet); a pack that isn't engaged is a
  point on the sand (no robots drawn until it comes within 130 m, only its dust plume nearby and a mark at the screen's
  edge); packs and raids never go for the convoy; the drawn dust plume only shows within 260 m; pacing judged from a
  headless walk and still shots, not played. The convoy lab has craters and death physics since 2026-11-01 (as the combat labs).
- **Expedition step 1 is rough:** recovery is instant (a fade, no helicopter, nothing can hit you while waiting);
  salvage pays scrap only (the at-risk list takes items, nothing drops them yet); fuel is a slider, not a property of the
  power pack item; the return point assumes a straight walk at walk pace (tall dunes slow the walker uphill, so far
  out on dunes the estimate runs optimistic; `homePace` and `homeMargin` are the knobs); sleeping camps stand frozen
  mid-pose and a camp that gives up freezes where it is rather than walking home; robots never go for the convoy and
  the turrets track awake ones without firing; POIs the road reaches after they're built stay (on a pad by the road);
  no convoy upgrades yet (radar range, packs, speed); judged only from still shots headless, not played.

- **Sheet hits are rough in places:** the slug's line runs from the arm's muzzle (about 2 m off the mech's centre)
  to the aim, so a line of targets straight out from the mech's centre is crossed at a slant (the aim line on the sand
  shows the real line); targets without a sheet of their own (vehicles, mechs, the pilot, wrecks) go through a fan of
  15 rays and back-rays, so their chord is approximate; the drawn slug flies straight from the muzzle toward 1.6 m over
  the aim and can cut visually through a crest the sheet passes over; enemies still shoot thin rays; the hand-held
  light minigun's 0.3 m comes from its pierce stat until items carry penetration. Feel judged from headless stills
  (8-16 s a frame here), not played.

## Changes

### 2026-11-14 · Gear comparison shows what the whole mech gets, soft limits counted (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-5rk08q`)
- `src/items/compare.ts` (pure): `compareFit` fits the item into a copy of the loadout as a click would (`fitWithHeld`), resolves both and lists what changes: sustained DPS of all weapons, walk, sprint, speed-up and turn after leg load, load and the legs' best load, hull, armour, reactor and the biggest energy shot, cargo cells, radar, power, then the per-gun details; warnings when a fit goes over or back under the legs' best load, or makes the mech underpowered or clears it; what comes off. `slotFor` is the slot a click picks (the inventory's equip uses it too).
- Inventory card (`src/ui/inventory.ts`): the old "+N dps · +N power vs fitted" line is now this comparison, into the slot a click would fit; dragging an item over a doll slot it fits shows the comparison for that slot. Bay hover (`src/ui/bay.ts`) uses the same module (gains DPS, speed-up and the warnings).
- Checked: `tools/compare-check.ts` (new: Stilt legs under a Siege hull, Siege legs 4.57 → 4.80 m/s walk, Rampart → 5.00, "back under" warning; sunder on a Salvager warns underpowered, a capacitor bank clears it; 115 base-slot fits where every card number equals the fitted mech's and the loadout is untouched); typecheck; `npm test`.

### 2026-11-14 · Loot slot mix: every slot drops, shoulder launchers more often (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-5qd0w5`)
- Keith felt only weapons dropped and wasn't sure he'd seen a shoulder weapon. Measured (40k field drops): weapons 38% (+ weapon quads' own gun drops on top), Protocols 20%, legs/hull/sensor/pack/waist ~6% each, shoulder launchers 6.6% (five of them, ~1.3% each). Cause: one pool picked by base, and 28 weapon bases outweighed everything.
- Now a random part or weapon picks its slot first (`kindWeapon` 30, `kindShoulder` 20, legs/hull/sensor/pack/waist 10 each; group "Loot slot mix", Loot topics), then a base in it by the bases' drop weights. Shoulder launchers' drop weights up (rocket 1.5, missile pod 1.5, mortars 1.2, napalm 1). Measured after: weapons 24%, Protocols 20%, shoulder 16% (launchers 9.7%), each other slot ~8%.
- Files: `src/items/generate.ts` (`kindWeights`, `rollItem`), `src/items/bases.ts`, topic lists in `src/expedition/loop.ts` and the loadout, convoy, inventory labs; new `tools/lootmix-check.ts` (each slot ≥ 6%, weapons ≤ 40%, launchers ≥ 8%, every droppable base turns up). Checked: `npm test` 110/110.

### 2026-11-14 · Weapons lie horizontal in the inventory (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-gbjf4z`)
- Every weapon's grid footprint is wider than tall: hull guns swapped (rail 8×2, autocannon 5×2, rocket launcher 4×3...), held 3×2, long held 4×2, swords 4×1 / 3×1 / 4×2, shoulder gear 3×2 (`src/items/gridSize.ts`). Pictures were already drawn tall and turned when flat, so they lie along the footprint with no new art; the doll tile no longer turns them again. Old saved layouts: `syncGrid` re-places any item whose size changed, so weapons just land in the first free spot. Checked: `grid-check` updated, full `npm test` 108/108. Open for Keith: the held 2×2→3×2 and shoulder 2×2→3×2 growth (sliders, Item sizes).

### 2026-11-14 · Inventory: Protocol sockets back, socket pips, half-screen doll, Select and Scrap selected (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-31xd5f`)
- Sockets in the inventory again: drag a Protocol onto an item with a free socket (grid or doll; the ones that take it light green), or click it to socket it into the picked slot's item (else the first fitted item it fits). The panel under the doll shows the picked slot's item (or the one selected item) with its sockets: Remove, and the Protocols on hand that fit.
- Socket pips on every item with sockets, in grids and on doll tiles: filled in the Protocol's rarity colour, hollow when free.
- Size: the doll takes Doll height (0.5) of the screen's height; text grows with it (×1 to ×1.4); grid cells are Cell size (now 36) on a 1080-tall screen, scaled ×0.8 to ×1.6 with the height. The panel spans the screen (over the tuning panel, below 2200 px wide). Phones: one scrolling column.
- The scrap bin (drop twice) is gone: Select (or Shift / Ctrl + click) picks items, Select all / Select shown (with a Show chip: a targeted bulk pick), Scrap selected banks them aboard (a second click if any is epic, legendary or holds Protocols), Drop selected leaves them on the sand in the field.

### 2026-11-14 · Longer walking strides (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-s1b9gi`)
- Keith: walking steps look too short on every leg (the sprint's are fine). Not a sim regression: the walker walks the same as on Nov 1 (same step lengths at 1/60 and 1/120). The cause in game: fitted leg items rolled their own short strides (0.45 m per m/s; Siege 0.36) over the leg types' (Rampart 1.5, Keith 2026-10-27) in every lab with a loadout, and the Stilt's legs, near straight at their hip height, hurried every step. Now leg items roll around their leg type's stride (old saved items are carried over to the new range on resolve), Stilt 1.0 per m/s (cap 7.5 m, was 0.5), Siege 0.8 (cap 6 m, was 0.36), and a new `walkCrouch` (0.15 m, Gait group; 0.18+ put a dashing toe into concrete in walker-check) lowers the hips at walk speed so the legs reach the longer step (walking straight on only: it eases out sideways and up climbs, which `response-check` and `slope-check` caught); the run's crouch takes over above it and the sprint is untouched. Walk cycle (two steps) at each legs' walk speed after the speed retune (5 / 5.2 / 4.8 m/s), in game: Rampart 4.5 → 6.1 m (2.2 → 1.6 steps/s), Stilt 3.3 → 5.8 m (3.2 → 1.8), Siege 3.8 → 5.4 m (2.6 → 1.8). Faster legs (the speed retune) cap at `strideMax` and step quicker beyond it.
- Files: `src/mech/walkerTuning.ts` (`walkCrouch`), `walker.ts` (hip target), `legTypes.ts`, `src/items/bases.ts`, `src/items/loadout.ts` (`strideNow`), `tools/walker-check.ts` (drop settle allows the walk crouch), `tools/duel-check.ts` (the long duel runs 120 s, was 90: seed 21's rival now first pushes at about 100 s; a push is a per-second chance). Checked: walker-check, a step-length probe, typecheck, `npm test`.

### 2026-11-14 · Capacitor-gated firing: heavy energy weapons need a reactor big enough for their shot (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-6rzkz1`)
- Second soft limit (`docs/proposal-soft-limits.md`). A weapon's **need** (`energyNeed` in `src/weapons/energy/energySim.ts`): the sunder beam's cost at the press, the nova lance's full charge; per-shot weapons and beams aren't gated. Over the reactor's capacity (`EnergyPool.cap`): a sunder beam never starts (flag `under`), a lance's charge stops at capacity / need and fires at that power. Energy free (Energy slider 0): never gated.
- Sunder beam 2.5 → **3.5 bars**: the Rampart (4) and Siege (5.5) hulls fire it, the Salvager and Cuirass (3) can't, unless a **capacitor bank** (shoulder) adds its new **+1.5 bars of capacity** (`capBonus`, slider `capCapacity`).
- HUD: the reactor bar has a gold line at the biggest fitted shot; red when the reactor can't hold it, flashing while one is held. Inventory figures: Reactor cap / need ("underpowered"). Bay summary: Biggest energy shot.
- Rival kit: a rolled heavy energy weapon the rival's reactor can't hold becomes a nova lance (no extra draw).
- Checked: `energy-check` gating section (underpowered sunder never starts and spends nothing; just enough starts; free isn't gated; lance on half the energy charges to 0.5 and fires at 0.5; Rampart 4 ≥ 3.5, Salvager 3 < 3.5, Salvager + bank 4.5); `npm test`.

### 2026-11-14 · Leg load: a soft weight limit instead of "can't fit" (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-6rzkz1`)
- Plan for soft limits (this and capacitor-gated firing next): `docs/proposal-soft-limits.md`.
- Legs get a best load (`carry`, t) and a strain (`strain`): Rampart 20 t, Stilt 16, Siege 27, Hover 15 (strain 1.5). The load is everything fitted above the legs (`legLoad` in `src/items/loadout.ts`); over it, walk and sprint × (1 − 0.5 × share over × strain), speed-up (0.8) and turn (0.6) likewise, never under 40%. Applied in `resolve()`, so the player, rivals and the duel sim all get it. Sliders: group Leg load (Loadout and Legs topics). At or under the best load nothing changes (the default mech carries 17.5 / 20 t).
- The hover legs' hard 6 t hull limit (`liftProblem`, stat `lifts`) is gone: any hull fits them (Rampart hull: walk × 0.87, Siege hull × 0.75). The rival kit leaves about 9 t of the legs' best load for weapons when it picks a hull.
- The inventory's figures show Load (t / best t, −% when over); the bay summary has Load, Legs' best load and Load: speed ×.
- Checked: `loadout-check` (leg load section: default under, monotonic, floor, 10% over = 5% slower, old saves fall back on the base), `hover-check` (any hull fits, heavy ones slower); `duel-check`'s "every energy weapon lands damage" now takes the best of up to four opponents (the lance went from 1360 to 0 against a rival 1% slower: one duel is chaotic); `npm test`.

- Keith: "a winding up noise ... like a capacitor charging. And then they fire." Held, the arc caster charges for `arcSpool` 0.5 s before its first zap (free, no energy), a capacitor whine climbing in pitch with the charge (a seamless loop, `arcWhine`) and a blue-white ball swelling at the muzzle with sparks crackling off it, the gun trembling. Rivals know the delay (`spinUp`). Sliders: Weapons › Arc caster (Spool-up, Spool drains in, Charge whine level, Charge glow size).

### 2026-11-14 · Robots and bandits in the sim worker move every tick again (Keith: swarms updating at ~1 Hz on his PC; Claude on `claude/fix-swarm-1hz`)
- Found (convoy lab `?siege=80&manual`, Keith's PC): the worker stepped and answered every tick, but the view's positions only changed every ~40 ticks. The swarm drawers' `SlotInterp.restore()` (src/look/treeInterp.ts) put the last tick's capture back after each drawn frame, so a worker snapshot landing between a tick and the next drawn frame was wiped; only a snapshot with no drawn frame before the next tick survived. Main-thread readers (aim, hits, camps) saw the stale view too. In since stage 6d (#294) met the worker swarms; `?noworker` and the swarm lab were fine.
- Fix: `restore()` puts back what the arrays held before `apply()`; `swarmBetweenTicks` takes a snapshot stamp (`snapStamp`, counted by `RobotsView.take`/`InfantryView.take`, skipped by the packers) and for a worker swarm captures when a snapshot lands, blending by the time since then over the time between snapshots.
- Checked: probe of robot positions every 100 ms now matches `?noworker`; `tools/treeinterp-check.ts` has a write-after-capture case (fails on the old restore); `npm test` 108/108.

### 2026-11-13 · Sim spikes near robot raids: big craters land over ticks, the worker's crater redraws read a cache (Keith: "sim times were getting up over 80ms on the convoy when robot raids were nearby"; Claude on `claude/project-thread-dau506`)
- Found (convoy lab `?siege=80&manual&norender&dt=0.0333`, headless, CPU profiles of the page and the worker): (1) a big robot's death crater (`fireRobotDeathBlast` → `bf.crater`, 16-24 m of radius, up to 25,600 lattice nodes and 17 new tiles) was fitted in one call, 60-270 ms, on the main thread ("swarm camps" / "combat rules" stage) and again in the worker's ground copy; (2) since the terrain patches went to the worker (step 2) its crater patches read the dunes and every modifier again for each vertex (the main thread's patch cache was not carried over): slumping craters redrew 150-250k vertices every 5 s, 2-3 s of worker time, the robots' answers up to 41 frames late.
- `src/world/craters.ts`: `add()` queues a crater over `craterStepNodes` / 4 nodes (new slider, Craters, 12000) or on top of a queued one; `fitSteps` (the old `fit` as a generator: yields the work each part took) is stepped by `update()` to the budget and lands through `changed`; `invalidate()` starts a begun one over (rushed after more than two); `settle()`, `pending`, `stats.deferred`. The fit's allowed rise per pair of neighbours is worked out once (four `Float64Array`s) instead of a closure called ~300 times a node. Same crater to the bit either way.
- `src/world/chunkFill.ts`: `groundVertexCached` and `newPatchBase` (moved from `chunks.ts`), `PatchCache` (64 chunks, least lately used out); `patchBlocks(…, cache, dentsOnly)`. `src/sim/worker.ts`: dents-only patches read it; a chunk's cache is dropped when it is built again or a road patch touches it, all on a new epoch (the main thread's rule). `src/game/combatRules.ts`: no `onGround` for a deferred crater (it comes at landing). Convoy HUD: craters on their way.
- Numbers (that siege, 60 s, cloud container, about 2-3× Keith's PC): worst sim tick in the fight 145-231 → 25-55 ms (the nova windows 76-182 → 21-29); sim worker average 12.1 → 7.4 ms, worst in the fight 189-322 → 25-80 ms.
- Checked: typecheck; full `npm test`; crater-check's new part (3 big craters and a blast on each: ground holds still until landing, 0 m off fitting at once, at most 12,288 ground reads a tick, start-over and rush); groundmirror-check with a big crater every 90 frames near the plough (0 of 115,200 samples differ); terrainworker-check with the stand-in worker on the cache (0 heights differ). Not seen on a GPU.
- Open for Keith: the crater appearing 0.3-0.6 s after a big walker's nova (Craters › Big craters: work a tick; 0 is the old way). Left: small craters still cost 5-35 ms there when they make new tiles (hardness reads 17 modifier points each; also 14% of the main tick through the walkers' foot sink), one 47 ms "raiders and fights" tick early in the siege.

### 2026-11-07 · Architecture stage 4.3: the robot swarm's state in the ECS (Claude)
- The host's world (`rules.world`) has a fixed capacity; a `RobotSwarm` reserves a block of 1024 entity indices in it and its arrays for position, heading, velocity, health, pace, kind, state, size, variant, weapon, uid and frozen are views of the block's columns (Transform, Velocity, Health, Status, new `Robot`), so there's one copy and no sync. An entity lives from a robot's spawn to its death; PrevTransform is copied at each update; Team and Collider are written as the register is published; the sim worker's view relinks after each snapshot. Proposal and notes in `docs/handoff-architecture.md`. `src/ecs/world.ts` (capacity, reserve, createAt, at, attach), `src/combat/robotWorld.ts` (new), `robots.ts`, `robotsRemote.ts`, `combatRules.ts`, `fronts/robots.ts`, `summon/allies.ts`; `robotEntities.ts`, `src/ecs/slots.ts` removed.
- Checked: a scripted 2400-tick camp fight hashes the swarm's snapshot identically before and after (own world and shared world); new `tools/robotworld-check.ts` (views alias columns, entity per live robot, stale ids, two swarms' blocks apart, a view relinks); full `npm test`. Nothing visible should change.

### 2026-11-06 · Big walkers' legs and reactor are targets (Keith asked; Claude on `claude/project-thread-2vnrf3`)
- Each of a big walker's four legs has its own health (0.3 of the walker's, `robotBigLegHp`): rounds on a leg (thigh, shin, foot; blasts at its feet too) wear it down as well as their usual share on the body. Shot through, it breaks below the knee (a charred stub, sparks and a crack, `onLegLost` event): the walker loses 8% health, walks at ×0.55 per lost leg and leans 7° toward the stump; two gone and it falls toward the stumps, a kill, and its reactor goes up as it tips (#236). Wrecks keep the stumps.

Rounds through it count ×2.5 through 0.35 of the armour, ahead of the disc in front of it; a kill through it breaches the reactor: it goes up 0.35 s later, standing (an airburst at full height). Rounds aimed at the body's centre never graze it. Sheets (the player's rounds) strike it by their flight line; the flat rail can't reach it.

### 2026-11-06 · Fewer ground lookups in fights near the convoy (Keith's frame drops; Claude on `claude/project-thread-pxoczn`)
- The ground height function was ~45% of the convoy siege's sim (the coordinator's assessment, `wasm-assessment/` in the project files). Callers that asked for the same ground over and over now don't; the ground everything stands on is unchanged.
- Death physics floor (`src/physics/deaths.ts`): the nodes sit on a fixed lattice, so a floor moved along reuses the heights it shares with the last one, and `groundChanged(box)` resamples only the box (a crater used to resample the whole 120 m floor; boxes off the floor are ignored). The convoy lab now also tells it about the plough's cuts. `tools/deaths-check.ts` checks every node against the height function after a move and after a crater (fails without the box resample).
- Escort planning (`src/world/heightMemo.ts`, new): the escorts' line, lane and steepness reads use a remembered copy of the ground (exact heights at nodes every 1.5 m, bilinear between, kept 3 s or until the convoy lab invalidates a changed box). Only scores routes; trucks still drive on the exact ground.
- Convoy cars (`src/convoy/platform.ts`): the plane under a car's supports is fitted every 4th step (the springs smooth it anyway). Trucks (`src/vehicles/car.ts`): the ground under the middle is read once a step, not twice.
- Checked (cloud box, convoy `?siege&manual&norender`): ground samples 3,625 → 2,594 a frame; deaths 0.54 → 0.24 ms, convoy 0.42 → 0.24 ms (noisy box). npm test 91/91.

### 2026-11-06 · Shadows: every moving part casts through one batch (Keith's frame drops near the convoy; Claude on `claude/project-thread-pxoczn`)
- Keith's PC profile had the shadow pass at ~17% of the frame: the renderer took every bolt, panel and gun part of every mech, escort and car into the sun's shadow map one by one (about 250 in the convoy siege, more than the camera draws).
- `src/look/shadowProxies.ts` (run by `createApp` before each render): every plain shadow-casting mesh (one material, no alpha or vertex nodes, not instanced or skinned, not mirrored) becomes an instance of a shadow-only `BatchedMesh` per shadow side on layer 4 (`SHADOW_PROXY_LAYER`, which the sun and the headlamps' shadow cameras see, the camera doesn't), and stops casting itself. Each frame the instances take their meshes' world matrices and visibility; the batch culls each part against each shadow camera. `castShadow` still reads and writes for the code. A full batch is replaced by a bigger one (the renderer keeps its first matrix texture). `createApp` now brings world matrices up to date once before the batches copy them, so the render skips that pass.
- Checked: the same held frame with the batches and with every part casting is pixel-identical in the swarm lab lineup and the convoy lab at night (`?out&set=time:22`); the convoy siege differs only in a few FX pixels (none over 40/255), also with the batch regrown from a tiny start. Convoy `?siege&manual` (cloud box, WebGL): shadow-pass objects 250 → 81, their CPU time down ~1.3 ms; the batches' update 1.27 ms of which 0.79 is the matrix pass the render did anyway. The HUD's draw count barely moves (the backend still issues one GPU draw per part). npm test 89/89.
- `?noproxy` turns it off to compare; HUD details: "shadow batches". Probes: `window.__shadowBatches.bypass`, `window.__app.hold` (skip the ticks so frames redraw the same moment).

### 2026-11-06 · New shaders build in the background: no multi-second freeze when new things first come into view (Keith: "a four-second hitch" venturing out from the convoy; Claude on `claude/project-thread-yo1r0n`)
- Cause: three builds a render pipeline the first time each material/object combination draws, and in a normal frame it asks the GPU synchronously, so the frame waits for the driver's compile (slow on Windows). The convoy lab builds about 140 at load and 50 more the first time the field's kinds (camps, robots, salvage, POIs, their shadows) come into view. With drawing off (`?norender`) the convoy lab out to the field had no frame over 200 ms: the sim was never the hitch.
- `src/core/asyncShaders.ts` (from `createApp`): every pipeline takes three's async path (the one `compileAsync` uses: `createRenderPipelineAsync` on WebGPU, parallel compile on WebGL 2); three skips an object until its pipeline is ready, so new things appear a few frames late instead of the game freezing. The dent layer's ping-pong passes stay sync (`buildSync`, `src/fx/dents.ts`). `?syncshaders` is three's old behaviour, to compare.
- The HUD's details have a `shaders` line (pipelines built, ms spent in frames, the shader code generation part, worst frame), and a spike entry names `new shaders N (code X ms, GPU Y ms)` when a frame built any.
- Checked: typecheck, `npm test`; convoy lab `?out&webgl` headless: the same 192 pipelines and the same draw calls with and without `?syncshaders` (everything still draws). Not checkable here: the WebGPU timing on Keith's PC (the cloud box's software WebGPU can't run the lab).
- Open: three's shader code generation (JS) still runs in the frame; if the spike log shows `code` in the hundreds of ms, the next step is warming the field's kinds at load.

### 2026-11-06 · Nothing you kill is thrown back at you (Keith: "the handheld minigun has negative knockback", seen after the Inferno and Cryo projectors; Claude on `claude/project-thread-kgyy0q`)
Two causes, neither a value carried between weapons. (1) Up close a held gun's muzzle (3-4 m out) is past the enemy, so rounds aimed from it flew back toward the mech: in the swarm lab up to 16 of 134 light-minigun rounds in 8 s went back against the barrel (dot to -0.93), and their kills were thrown at the mech. Fights with the short-range flamers and freeze rays are close, which is why it showed after them. Now `src/weapons/shotLine.ts` moves a point-blank round's start back along the barrel (up to `shotPullBack` 4 m) so it flies outward (`mechUnit.ts`: minigun, round guns, flamers, freeze rays); rails and beams never aim back against the barrel. Swarm lab after the swap: 0 backward rounds. (2) Burn, heat, pool and cold ticks hit with a bare "up", which the robots and bandits turned into a throw along their own heading, at the mech they were charging (12 of 15 burn kills flew toward the mech). Now ticks carry the round's way in plan (`burn.ts` `pushDir`, `chill.ts`, `shots.ts`), and a kill with no way in plan drops where it stands (`robots.ts`, `infantry.ts` kill). Also: a weapon swap now resets the arsenal side's leftovers (sword swing counters, rail whine and meter, the arm missile pod's missiles and values). Checks: new `tools/pointblank-check.ts`, `tools/weaponswap-check.ts` (702 swaps between the 27 picks, each against a fresh fit); full `npm test`.
### 2026-11-06 · Ground chunks stream in a few rows a frame: no ~25 ms hitch per chunk (Keith: "getting down to like 20 FPS" fighting near the convoy; Claude on `claude/project-thread-pxoczn`)
- Keith's PC (`/mnt/project-files/convoy-perf-hud.md`): the convoy lab is CPU-bound (GPU under 3 ms); every few seconds the "ground" stage took 25-29 ms for several frames in a row, one whole chunk (65 x 65 vertices) a frame whatever the budget. `TerrainStreamer` (`src/world/chunks.ts`) now fills a chunk's rows under `budgetMs` (4 ms) and finishes it over the next frames; chunks within 1.5 chunks of the focus (on screen) are still built at once, and a build is finished outright after 30 frames. A patch or rebuild landing on rows already filled winds the build back to the first row it touches, so a chunk comes out exactly as one built in one go. Dirty rebuilds work the same way (the old chunk draws until the new one is done).
- New `tools/chunks-check.ts`: chunks built a row a frame, with a patch over a part-built chunk and a rebuild on one not yet started, match chunks built at once to the last bit (fails without the wind-back). Checks: chunks, crater, dents, terrain; full `npm test`.

With the slower convoy (2.2 m/s, pitch follows speed) the loop already plays at 0.81 of its own pitch against 0.94 at 3.3 m/s, about 2.5 semitones down; this takes it to 0.78, about 3 semitones under what Keith heard. `src/convoy/convoySound.ts`. Checked: typecheck, full `npm test`.

### 2026-11-06 · Fortress waves: big, growing inward, half out through the gates (Keith: "way more bandits... giant waves... swarming out of the gates"; Claude on `claude/project-thread-exzrkr`)
- Waves were 6 every 12 s out of the spawners, 60 at most. Now 18 per wave plus 8 per ring inward (outer 18, middle 26, keep 34), the first with the alarm, then every 20 s, up to 300 per fortress. ### 2026-11-06 · Held weapons aim on round behind the shoulder (Keith: "the elbow rolls up and then the wrist kind of almost inverts... shoot almost directly behind the hull, maybe 175 degrees 170 degrees"; Claude on `claude/project-thread-z2lp4z`)
- Sprinting, a held gun's arm now tracks out to 170° off the hull's facing on its own side (`runArmOut`, was 95°; slider to 178). Past side-on the arm rolls over (Your mech › Held weapons › Arm roll): from `armRollFrom` 90° over `armRollOver` 45° the elbow swings out and then up (`armPoleUp` 4: bent about 76 to 83° up off the shoulder-fist line, almost straight up), the reach shortens to 0.7 of the arm (`armReachBack`), the fist sits on the aim line (`armDropBack` 0), the forearm follows the aim less (`armAlignBack` 0.2), the gun turns over on its barrel (`armRollWrist` 180°, the sign picks which way), and the hull leans away from that arm up to 8° (`armRollLean`, looks only; the arms aim in the hull's frame so they stay on target). Outside its arc an arm now goes to the nearer end round the circle, so a target just past dead behind keeps it at its back limit instead of swinging it round the front. `src/mech/upper.ts` (`armRoll`, the arc clamp), `src/mech/rampart.ts` (`reachArm`, hull lean). Checked: `upper-check` (sprinting: 160° left fires, arm at 163°; dead behind: none fire, arms at ±170°; 175° right: left arm stays at 170°), a node probe of the arm (elbow bend 83° up at 120°, 76° at 170°, fist 2 m behind the root), full `npm test` 85/85. ### 2026-11-05 · Flamer stream as one continuous arcing line (Keith, with a flame tank reference: "a line that you'd expect to see ... as it builds pressure it arcs out further"; Claude on `claude/project-thread-q7b0ap`)
- Look only; damage unchanged. Each pouring nozzle lets go of a fuel point every 0.025 s (`src/fx/flameStream.ts`, pure); each flies its own ballistic arc (gravity, drag), aimed to come down on the aim, so a swung stream bends like a hose. The points joined in order, with the nozzle at the near end, are drawn as one tube (`src/fx/flameTube.ts`, a CPU-built ring mesh): 0.06 m at the nozzle widening to 0.75 m, noise running down its length, blue at the nozzle, white-hot yellow, orange, deep orange at the end, drawn over in 0..1 colours. Pressure builds over 0.7 s while held: the line starts at 35% of its reach and slower, and arcs out to the aim. Where points land, ground fires splash (60% of them, 0.75 m) and burn with flames and smoke; past the reach they burn out in the air and rise as fireballs into smoke. Flames shed off the line as it goes, smoke from its far end. Loose gobs are off by default (Flame stream › loose gobs per jet).
- Sliders: Flame stream › Pressure builds over, Reach at no pressure; Flame look › Line width, Line width at the nozzle / end, Line lumps, Line point every, Flames off the line, Ground fire where it lands, End fire size.
- Checked: `flamefx-check` (new: the line aimed 12 m lands at 12.0 m, aimed 60 m burns out at 23.3 m for the 22.5 m reach, widest gap between joined points 0.67 m, nothing under the sand, lines gone after they stop); node probe two Infernos swinging: 0.31 ms, 370 particles. A failed check runs once more on its own before it counts: dents-check's "coarse grid beside the plough" can fail under load, because the patch fill has a ms budget (it passes on its own; worth a look: under load the coarse grid lags the chunks' by up to 1.7 m).

### 2026-11-05 · Flamer look rebuilt: burning fuel thrown under gravity, splashing or burning out and rising (Keith: "start again"; Claude on `claude/project-thread-q7b0ap`)
- Look only; the damage (the gun's rays, the stream's reach, the splash pools) is as it was. Each gob is real ballistic fuel: FireGuns aims it to come down on the aim (`lobVy`, at most a 35° lob, out to 1.6× the reach) at the fuel speed, from the end of the nozzle cone, and gives it fuel for 1.1× the flight to the reach. Flying, it falls under gravity (9.8) with little drag, streaks along its arc by speed and breaks up into branches (1.2/s). Coming down still burning, it splashes (2 droplets thrown on and up) and burns on the sand for 1.2 s as a low ground fire licking flames up; aimed past the reach, its fuel runs out in the air about the reach out and it lets go of the arc, rising (6 m/s²) as a fireball that grows ×3 and turns to smoke. Smoke only from fire burning down. The swarm, the attractors and the gather/flow sliders are gone. Freeze rays use the same arcs (mist that settles or fades).
- Sliders (Flame look): Lobs out to, Burns for, Breaks up, Splash droplets, Burnt-out fireball rises for / grows, Grows in flight, Ground fire burns / grows, Stretched by speed; Flame stream › gravity and air drag on the fuel.

- Projectiles: Accelerator (+40% shell speed, −10% damage).

### 2026-11-04 · Double click: the vertical sword cut (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-pooipi`)
- A second click within `swordDoubleTime` (0.3 s) of the first, while the cut still drives in (or up to `swordVertLate` 0.2 of the way into its cut), turns it into a vertical cut: from the forehand (cut 1) an **overhead** cut straight down the aim (`V`), from the backhand (cut 2) a **rising** cut straight up it (`U`). Double-clicked before a queued cut starts, that cut starts vertical. The drive is the same move, so it switches at the last moment; it ends up where the broad cut would (under / cocked), so combos carry on. Hold-to-keep-swinging and Space power cuts unchanged (they stack).
- Harder, narrower: damage × `swordVertDamage` 1.6, cleave budget × `swordVertCleave` 0.6, its sheet reaches only as far as the blade does over the ground (raised: near the hand), tip swept 0.2 m across vs the broad cut's 12 m. Pose sliders: `swordVertHigh` 75°, `swordVertLow` −40°, `swordVertIn` 1.1 m, `swordVertCloseIn` 0.6 m, `swordVertTime` 0.9 (Weapons › Sword vertical cut; the Sword power group now shows in the Weapons topic too). Pose lab: Overhead and Rising clips, keys vStart/vMid/vEnd, uStart/uMid/uEnd.
- `src/weapons/sword.ts` (clips V, U, `turnVertical`), `arsenal.ts`, `swordPoses.ts`, `labs/pose/`, `tools/sword-check.ts` (double click from rest and close, late click, mid-combo, off, edge leads, clear of the torso, line not arc), `tools/pose-check.ts`. Flak cannon gets a model (`build/roundGuns.ts` `flakHull`: a pan magazine on top behind the shoulder, three short barrels); longshot rifle too (`sniperHull`: long receiver, box magazine and buffered butt behind, barrel to 4 m, was 5.8).

- Keith: prints showed as paint only, never in the mesh. Read the dent texture back in the walker lab (WebGL): shaped brushes wrote compression but zero depth and berm, the oval wrote both. The dent pass read the brush rows as nodes first used inside the shaped branch of an If, so the depth maths after it read them unset. The rows, along/across and the rim wobble are now variables set before the If. After: a Rampart print is 11 cm deep in the sole's shape (two toes and the heel), berm 11 cm.
- `src/fx/dents.ts`. npm test; readback in the walker lab.

### 2026-11-02 · Footprints press the mesh; skids in the foot's shape (Keith; Claude on `claude/project-thread-j1a1o6`)
- Keith: the shaped prints read as painted on, and skids didn't use the foot brush. Cause: the detail patch's vertices are 25 cm apart and low-passed one vertex apart, so a foot-sized print reached about one vertex (probe: 1.4 vertices past half depth for the Rampart's sole, 3.9 for the old oval); the rest was per-pixel shading. Now the patch has a dense middle (`patchFineArea` 32 m, `patchFineSub` 2: 12.5 cm, the ground bilinear between samples, the dent low-pass at the local spacing): about 15–22 vertices pressed per print. 148k to 263k patch vertices, GPU only (no extra CPU ground sampling; not in the shadow pass with the shadow terrain on). Skids stamp the foot's sole along the slide (`GroundMarks.skid`, every 10 cm, Dash furrow depth), the oval furrow without a shape.
- `src/world/detailPatch.ts`, `src/fx/dents.ts` (sliders), `src/fx/groundMarks.ts`, `src/game/mechUnit.ts`. npm test; walker lab compiles and draws in WebGL (software GPU).

### 2026-11-01 · Robots lab performance: crater ground patched once a frame, dunes cached under the dents (Keith reported low fps; Claude on `claude/project-thread-i1307j`)
- Cause: the excavators' beams dig trenches (up to 60 craters a beam) and craters slump and heal, each asking the chunks to redo the ground in an overlapping box, every vertex re-reading the full dune function 9 times; the detail patch then refilled the same boxes. In a 250-robot fight that was about a quarter of the main thread (CPU profile, robots lab, ~45 s in).
- Fix, same look: `TerrainStreamer.queuePatch`/`flushPatches` (`src/world/chunks.ts`) merge a frame's changed boxes on a 4 m grid and finish each chunk once; the flush reads the ground under the dents from a per-chunk cache (dents are added last in `DuneField.sample`, so only they are re-read). `GroundWindow` (`src/world/groundWindow.ts`) keeps the fine dunes the same way for dents-only refills (`onRebuild`'s new `dentsOnly`). `combatLab.ts` queues and flushes after `bf.update`; the convoy's road patches still go through `patchArea` uncached. Robot part pools upload only the instances drawn (`robotsDraw.ts`; they hold ~47k, 3 MB a frame sent whole).
- Numbers (sandbox CPU, 20 s of a big fight): ground patching 5.7 s → 0.3 s, detail patch refills 2.4 s → 0.6 s; main thread idle 4.3 s → 7.6 s. Crater check: cached patch matches fresh chunks to 1.3e-6 m, 9× cheaper than patching each box; the window's dents-only refill matches a fresh fill.
- Not changed: the strider is ~150 separate meshes (left alone while its disc is being reshaped); Rapier ragdolls are the next biggest cost. Keith's damage note: weapon quads have 700 health (old mediums 220), slider Robots: weapon quads › Health.
- Checked: typecheck, crater, dents, terrain, biome, robots checks, full `npm test`.

### 2026-10-31 · Terrain pop at the top of the screen when moving north, fixed (Keith reported; agent on `wip/terrain-pop`)
- Cause: the fine ground patch round the focus (`src/world/groundWindow.ts`) moved its window at once but filled the rows that scrolled in under its budget, behind every queued refill, so its top edge drew rows left over from the far side of the window (up to 7 m off) for frames on end; near the convoy the plough's refills kept the queue long, so it was worst there. Now the window stores a lead (`patchLead`, 6 m, new slider in Ground dents) beyond what it draws, filled ahead under the budget; anything the lead hasn't reached when it comes into view is filled that frame. Refills of changed ground are merged per row (the plough's boxes overlap). The patch's coarse grid is copied from the chunks' own vertices (`TerrainStreamer.vertexAt`) instead of sampled again (about a third less fill CPU, and exactly the chunks'). The patch's edge blend is measured from the focus, so it glides instead of stepping a metre at a time (`detailPatch.ts`); `gpuGround` wraps at the storage size.
- Checked: `tools/dents-check.ts` gains: scrolling north at 12 m/s on no budget draws only filled samples (lead 0 and 6 m); beside the plough at 6 m/s no rows are filled at the last moment and the coarse grid is the chunks' exactly. `tools/patch-probe.ts` (new): before 255/600 frames drew wrong rows, worst 7 m; after, none from scrolling (what's left is the plough's own cut sweeping in over a few frames). Convoy lab runs in WebGL with no shader errors. Full `npm test` passes.

### 2026-10-31 · Grip comes back as the mech slows: no flat low-traction window after a sprint, dash or landing (Keith, agent on `wip/grip`)
- Keith: after the dash and after the sprint "a flat period of really low traction"; traction should return as the speed falls. Cause: letting go of the sprint (and a dash's or a landing's end) put the speed past the walk's into `skid`, which died at a fixed `landFriction` 3 m/s² whatever the stick did (up to ~3 s; 34 m of run-on). Now (`src/mech/walker.ts` `gripAt`, `walkSteer`, `runSteer`; `walkerTuning.ts`): sideways grip is one function of speed: the walk brake (40 m/s²) up to the Shift run + `gripFullOver` (0), the sprint's `boundGrip` × g at the legs' top while sprinting (the drift at speed unchanged), `gripCurve` 2 between; let go, the curve runs to the burst's speed + `gripLetGoOver` (0). The walk steers the whole motion (walk + skid) at that grip; past the Shift run along the stick it's taken off at the grip too. `landFriction` is now the least grip ("Skid: least grip"). The tapped dash's held line is untouched.
- Numbers (lateral response, m/s², 0.05 s windows; `tools/grip-probe.ts`): sprint let go, stick across: was 43 for 0.2 s then **3 flat for 1.9 s+** (10.7 → 7 m/s); now 8, 9, 10, 13, 16, 22, 31, 40 by 0.82 s (17.9 → 7.8 m/s). Stick off: was 3 flat ~2.9 s; now 8 → 40 by 0.62 s, stopped 0.8 s. Run-on with the stick held: 1.00 s / 11.6 m (was 3.14 s / 34.3 m). Sprint at its top: 6.9 both; at 15.4 m/s 7.1 (was 6.9). Dash: no change (0 through the held line, 150-180 in the stop, 40 after).
- Checked: dash check (new: after a sprint let go across / off / across later and a dash, the response never falls as speed falls, no flat stretch over 0.2 s under 90 % of full, full within 1.3 s; `gripAt` never rises with speed; the sprint's grip at its top = boundGrip × g), response, walker, slope (downhill 33° overreach 8.9 % vs 16.5 %), convoy (the launch-landing check now measures the whole motion, skid too, 0.1 s after touchdown: it was blind to a sideways skid), duel; full npm test.

### 2026-10-31 · Weapons and sounds stop when their owner dies, gets out or the round restarts (Keith's bug, agent on `wip/deathstop`)
Causes: (1) the minigun growl loops were one shared set indexed by gun, updated only while the player was alive and armed, so after the player died (or any update stopped) they kept playing at the last level for good; nothing ever stopped a loop; (2) a dead MechUnit returned from `step` before its weapons ran, so the arsenal froze mid-state: rail charge, coil glow and aim line, painter strips and rings, lasers, the pod channel (walk stayed slowed), feed overdrive, and rockets or shells in flight froze on the spot; a held rail would also have fired on release. Fix: `MinigunAudio` is per owner (`update(owner, ...)`, `release(owner)`, `tick(dt)` releases a voice not updated for 0.25 s); `Sfx` loops fade out on stop and a watchdog (`src/audio/loopWatch.ts`, run from `Sfx.update`) stops any loop not refreshed for 0.3 s (covers the party bus music and anything new); `Arsenal.stop()` (weapons cold: triggers let go without firing, all held effects dropped; also run every frame for a dead owner); `MechUnit.goCold()` on death, `reset` (duel round restart, respawn) and getting out of the mech, a dead unit keeps stepping its weapons cold so rounds in the air fly on; `GunStreams.clear()` on reset. Robots' beam charge already clears on death (`setState` to DOWN). Check: `tools/deathstop-check.ts` (in `npm test`): per-owner loops, silent owner released at 0.25 s, watchdog, 8 live weapon effects all cold within 0.5 s for death / stop / dead-while-held with 0 shots, rockets keep flying. Files: audio/minigunAudio.ts, audio/sfx.ts, audio/loopWatch.ts, weapons/arsenal.ts, game/mechUnit.ts, game/battlefield.ts (one line), fx/gunStreams.ts. Never run in a browser.

### 2026-10-31 · A crisp dash; a tap never sticks in the run; no drift after a dash; rails vs the dash (Keith, agent on `wip/dashsnap`)
- Keith: the dash felt slow to start and stop next to the new walk; a tap sometimes left him running in a run he couldn't leave; a drift during the dash or WASD; rail balance: no dash while a rail charges (the dash wins, the charge is lost). Before: peak 18 m/s 0.58 s after the press (the footwork built it), back to walk 0.5-0.7 s after, 12.2 m. Cause of the stuck run and the drift: the let-go short sprint re-boosted to its peak whenever a drift scrubbed speed, so with the stick off the dash's line it ran on at 18 m/s for ever. Now (`src/mech/dashSteps.ts` `dashBurst`, `walker.ts`, `walkerTuning.ts`): 90 % of 20 m/s 0.05 s after the press, full at 0.07, held to 0.49, at the walk 0.63 s after the press, 11.2 m from standing (11.3 walking, 11.1 dashing back); a stick turned mid-dash is under it 0.16 s after the burst (main: never). ### 2026-10-27 · Dash any way, any time on the ground: a backpedal into the side step; dash out of a dash (Keith, Claude)

Causes: from a fast short sprint the stretched legs pulled every beat early, so the run took over still going the old way, and the short sprint's end check fired before its boost (back to the walk at 3 m/s, the stick took it); a second dash was refused while the first's shove (1.05 s) or steps ran; starting a dash out of a run dropped the run's extra speed. Now (`src/mech/walker.ts`): a dash against the way it's going (a cutback, or over `dashPedalFrom` 2 m/s against it) backpedals first (`pedalSteer`, `pedalFeet`, `pedalLift`, `pedalAim`): still facing the old way, crouched and leaning back, it brakes at `dashPedalBrake` (70 m/s²) toward `dashPedalSpeed` (5) the new way, feet skidding past `dashPedalGrip` (5 m/s), stepping (the trailing foot through while braking, then back past the other; a stretched leg first) at least `dashPedalSteps` (2) times, then the side step round (`dashPlan`). A dash can start any time on the ground, mid-dash too (`Dash.start` only waits `dashGap`); all the speed it had goes in. New Backpedal sliders in Dash.

### 2026-10-27 · Letting go of the sprint, part 2: at top speed, turning and out of the gallop's landing stance (Keith's playtest, Claude)
- Keith: still dipping, less often. A wider probe (432 releases: flat and dunes, held 2/5/9 s, straight and weaving, into the walk and the Shift run, 12 timings each) found three more causes in `src/mech/walker.ts`: (1) the hurry read the stretch at the hips' current height, so hips sinking after a trailing foot hid it and nothing hurried (runaway to 0.38 m); now read at their wanted height off the run (`hipShort`). (2) After letting go, the step timing (cadence, planted share, landing spots) still blended the run's for `runBlend`; now `gaitK` is the run's share of the timing and is 0 once the run ends (the pose still blends on `runK`), and the planted share snaps to the walk's (the sprint's is under a half, so both feet were up together). ### 2026-10-27 · Letting go of the sprint no longer drops into the splits (Keith's playtest, Claude)
- Keith: "sometimes when blending between run/sprint the mech kind of crouches/falls as if it's doing the splits into the ground". Cause: on letting go the walk took over a foot the run had planted under the hips and held it for a whole walk stance (d × stride), at the run's slower cadence, with the reach hurry eased and scaled to `runHurry`; the foot trailed 3+ m behind and the reach cap pulled the hips down after it (to 0.83 m with Keith's stride, 1.74 m with the old). Fix in `src/mech/walker.ts`: `runEnd` sets the gait phase from where the rearmost planted foot already is along the walk's stance, and the hurry acts in full whenever the run gait is off (eased and scaled only while running).
- Checked (node probe, 24 release timings each): hips after letting go, flat, into the Shift run 2.32 m lowest (was 1.74, 96 frames under 2 m, now 0), into the walk 2.37 (was 0.91, 215 frames, now 0); on dunes the only dips left are landing squats after hops. npm test: everything passes with the fix alone; the slope check fails only with Keith's new values (below).

- What Keith saw uphill: the run's hull clearance (`runClear` 3 m over the ground `runLookAhead` ahead) held the hips past the legs' reach on a climb (4.3 m over a 15° slope, legs at 120-130% of their length, 45% of leg-frames out of reach), and the over-stretch hurry flicked the cadence between 1 and 2.5 times. Now: `runCadence()` adds `runClimbSteps` (0.1) steps/s per degree of climb along the line of run (2.6 steps/s on the flat, 3.4 at 10°, 4.0 at 20°; `runDescentSteps` 0 keeps it downhill) and shrinks the ground per stance with the stride; the run keeps `runHurry` (0.3) of the hurry, eased; the hips may rise no more than `runReachGive` (0.1 m) past what the feet carrying them reach (planted in the first half of a stance, landing spots against where the hips will be), eased over a stride; running, a landing spot's reach is judged from the running height (the run down a 10-15° slope hung its feet in the flight and dropped every 1.3 s). Flat run unchanged (same bob, flights, crest jumps). `src/mech/walker.ts` (`runCadence`, `body`, `aim`), `walkerTuning.ts` (Sprint group).
- Checked: typecheck; `slope-check` new section: sprint cadence on -10/0/10/20/25° planes rises with the climb, even intervals (within 3%), legs in reach climbing (1.5% at 10°, 0 above), no rise downhill; was 2.4-3.2 steps/s with 80-100% uneven intervals and 13-70% reach misses up 5-25°. `walker-check`: dune sprint reach misses 6.9 → 3.5% (Rampart), 5.3 → 4.1% (Stilt); ramp hull clearance 1.56/1.59/1.25 → 2.55/2.18/1.69 m (Rampart); into walls the hips rise at most 1.3 m (was 7.0); other results as before. ### 2026-10-26 · The sprint leans into turns (Keith's 21:54 notes; agent on `wip/sprint-feel`)
- Sprinting, the whole mech rolls into a turn by the sideways pull: `turnRoll` (1) × the true lean for speed × the line of run's turn rate, capped at `turnRollMax` (30°), eased at `turnRollEase` (6/s). The hull takes all of it (it isn't levelled like the hip roll), so the shoulders come over the inside; the body rolls about the outside hip and the hips move `turnLeanShift` (0.25) of a ground pivot toward the inside, so the inside leg folds short and the outside one reaches no further than upright. The drift (body yawed past the travel line, feet skidding with slide dust) was already there. `src/mech/walker.ts` (`turnLean`, `orient`, `hullRoll`; `carry` now turns the line of run too), `walkerTuning.ts` (Sprint group), `src/mech/rampart.ts`.
- Checked: typecheck; `walker-check` new section: hard weaves at top speed (Rampart and Stilt, flat and dunes) lean to the 30° cap and never past it, no foot under the ground, feet skid in the drift, reach misses no worse than the same weave upright (1.5 vs 3.4% flat, 3.1 vs 6.6% dunes); the sprint's 60 s dune run as before (6.9%, 5.1%). Stick strip from behind: `sf/turn-back.png` in the session scratchpad.

### 2026-10-24 · Rounds pierce and splash; small arms read small; mech tougher (Claude)
- Keith's playtest: swarms too hard to hit, the mech doesn't feel powerful. `Battlefield.fire` now carries a round through light bodies (`canPierce`: bandits, small robots; `roundPierce` 2, 70% kept each) and splashes swarm units near where it ends (`splash` on the swarm targets; `roundSplash` 1.6 m at `roundSplashShare` 0.5, half at the edge), so a near miss into the sand at their feet still counts. New "Rounds" group under Your mech. Hull 1000 → 1500; robot bite 1.5 → 0.8, small shot 2 → 1.2, big shot 15 → 10.
- Small arms read small: `FireOptions.hitScale` scales sparks, dust and grit, and below 0.6 leaves no crater (bandits and small robots 0.3, technicals 0.4, gun trucks 0.75, enemy mechs 0.7, tank 1.6).
- **Aim bug fixed:** the player's rounds flew along the barrel, whose pitch also carried the hull's walking lean, from a muzzle below the shoulder the guns aim from: they went about 2 m low at 20 m, into the sand in front of what you aimed at. Rounds now keep the barrel's heading (the guns' lag) but pitch to arrive at the aim's height (`mechUnit.ts`). Measured in the bandits demo: misses from the target centre went from 1.5-2.8 m (all low) to 0.2-1.2 m (the spread); kills per round about 1 in 3, was about 1 in 16. Bandit health 30 → 12. Pierced bodies show a puff (bandits) or sparks (small robots).
- Checked: typecheck; walker, upper, infantry, warband, robots, deaths, targeting, convoy, bandit-vat checks; headless miss and kill counts in the bandits lab.
